#### Synthesis Summary
14 material issue(s) require attention. Highest-priority issue: issue-001 (high) — Issue-001 is a high-severity material defect: the ontology cannot safely serve as a WMS/TMS/ERP integration baseline while allocation depends on current or nightly inventory quantity surfaces instead of time-scoped, source-authoritative availability records. Controlled deliberation did not leave a blocking unresolved disagreement for the highest-priority issue.

#### Material Issues
- issue-001 (high)
  - affected purpose: Use the ontology as the WMS/TMS/ERP integration concept baseline for order, inventory, shipment, and delivery concepts, especially authority and temporality. / Inventory and order fulfillment authority for WMS/TMS/ERP integration.
  - failure condition: During intraday order allocation, the ontology directs consumers to a daily InventoryAggregate snapshot while operational WMS and accounting ERP quantities may diverge until nightly reconciliation. / When an order is accepted or audited at a prior point in time, the ontology cannot reconstruct available or on-hand inventory from effective timestamps or movement history.
  - impact: The reference model can drive oversell, under-allocation, and audit disputes because it does not identify the valid inventory authority or validity time for allocation decisions. / The concept baseline cannot support allocation correctness, dispute analysis, or cross-system reconciliation because only current/nightly quantity surfaces are modeled.

- issue-005 (high)
  - affected purpose: WMS/TMS/ERP integration concept baseline for order, inventory, shipment, and delivery concepts.
  - failure condition: When one order has multiple lines or a line is partially fulfilled across multiple shipments, the ontology has no concept to bind the fulfilled quantity to the actual shipment or leg.
  - impact: Operational reconciliation and exception handling become non-actionable because order status, inventory movement, and shipment evidence cannot be traced at the unit where fulfillment actually occurs.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-001`, `OrderLine comment: no reference to actual cargo/segment fulfilling the line`, `relations `fulfills`: Shipment to Order only; note says no OrderLine-level mapping`, `Embedded materialized input: OrderLine attributes comment; relations `fulfills` note`
  - source lenses: coverage
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, accept_risk
  - problem definition: The fulfillment relation is scoped to Shipment -> Order and lacks a line-level allocation entity binding fulfilled OrderLine quantity to Shipment or DeliveryLeg.
  - problem framing: root_cause / next_step_blocker / needs_decision / must_close_before_next_stage / contested

- issue-014 (high)
  - affected purpose: Concept reference document for WMS/TMS/ERP integration across order, inventory, shipment, and delivery concepts.
  - failure condition: When one order is split across multiple shipments or one shipment contains selected lines from multiple orders, the ontology cannot identify which OrderLine quantity was fulfilled by which physical movement.
  - impact: Integration consumers cannot reliably reconcile OMS line status, WMS pick/pack records, TMS shipment contents, and carrier delivery events at the line level, reducing the ontology’s usefulness as a concept standard.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/structure.findings.yaml#structure-candidate-001`, `materialized-input.md: OrderLine has sku_ref and qty only, plus an explicit comment that no reference exists to the shipment/leg that fulfilled the line.`, `materialized-input.md: relations contains only fulfills from Shipment to Order and notes that OrderLine-level mapping is omitted.`, `materialized-input.md: Shipment.total_weight note says it is the sum of constituent OrderLine weights.`, `materialized-input.md: OrderLine attributes only include sku_ref and qty; comment states no reference from the line to the actual fulfilling shipment/leg. relations defines only fulfills from Shipment to Order with note that OrderLine-level mapping is not included.`
  - source lenses: structure
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, accept_risk
  - problem definition: Shipment composition and reconciliation require line-level mapping from OrderLine quantities to Shipment and optionally DeliveryLeg, but fulfillment is modeled only at order granularity.
  - problem framing: root_cause / next_step_blocker / needs_decision / must_close_before_next_stage / contested

  - failure condition: When DeliveryLeg instances such as hub-to-customer are interpreted through the `is_a: Shipment` declaration, they must also satisfy the narrower Shipment definition of warehouse-to-customer movement. / When a delivery has multiple legs, consumers cannot reconstruct the end-to-end route or associate leg-level tracking and locations with one parent shipment.

- issue-012 (medium)
  - affected purpose: 주문·재고·화물·배송 개념의 단위 정합성을 보장하는 통합 기준 문서 목적. / WMS/TMS/ERP 통합에서 SKU, 화물, 배송 계산의 공통 개념 기준
  - failure condition: SKU 중량이나 치수 원천이 kg/lb 또는 서로 다른 길이 단위를 섞어 제공되는 경우 Shipment.total_weight가 구성 OrderLine 중량의 합으로 계산된다. / kg/lb 또는 cm/in가 섞인 창고, 운송사, 국가권역, 요율 엔진을 추가하는 경우
  - impact: 동일한 total_weight 값이 어떤 단위의 합인지 알 수 없어 운송사, 창고, ERP 간 물류 수치 매핑과 검증이 신뢰하기 어렵다. / 동일 필드의 숫자가 시스템마다 다른 의미를 가져 기존 데이터와 새 데이터의 비교, 합산, 요율 계산 연속성이 약해집니다.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-003`, `materialized-input.md Sku.weight note`, `materialized-input.md Sku.dims note`, `materialized-input.md Shipment.total_weight note`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md: Sku.weight note "창고별 계량 관례(kg 또는 lb)를 그대로 쓴다."; Sku.dims note "단위는 소스 시스템마다 다르다."; Shipment.total_weight note "구성 OrderLine 중량의 합."`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-002`, `materialized-input.md:33-38`, `materialized-input.md:40-49`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:33-38,40-49`
  - source lenses: semantics, evolution
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - problem definition: The unit model is not evolvable because measurement unit is not part of the measurement value concept, making weight, dimension, and total-weight values unstable across systems and conventions.

- issue-013 (medium)
  - affected purpose: 배송 상태 모델과 WMS/TMS/ERP/캐리어 통합 상태 의미를 일관되게 제공하는 목적.
  - failure condition: 캐리어 이벤트를 내부 Shipment.status나 Order.fulfillment_status로 반영하는 연동을 둘 이상 구현할 때 각 연동이 자체 매핑을 만든다.
  - impact: delivered, in_transit, exception 같은 상태의 의미가 연동별로 달라져 배송 완료, 예외 처리, 고객 알림 기준이 불일치할 수 있다.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-004`, `materialized-input.md TrackingEvent.event_type note`, `materialized-input.md integrity_rules 상태 독립 관리`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md: TrackingEvent.event_type note "캐리어별 상태 코드. 자사 Shipment.status와의 매핑은 각 연동에서 알아서 한다."; integrity_rules "Order.fulfillment_status, Shipment.status, TrackingEvent.event_type는 각 시스템(OMS/TMS/캐리어)이 독립으로 관리한다."`
  - source lenses: semantics
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - problem definition: Carrier source event meanings and internal Shipment status meanings are not canonically mapped in the ontology, delegating status interpretation to external integration implementations.
  - problem framing: root_cause / next_step_blocker / fix_now / must_close_before_next_stage / observed

#### Synthesized Material Issue Explanations
- issue-001 (high): Issue-001 is a high-severity material defect: the ontology cannot safely serve as a WMS/TMS/ERP integration baseline while allocation depends on current or nightly inventory quantity surfaces instead of time-scoped, source-authoritative availability records.
  - issue stance agreement: 6/6
  - agreed or narrowed lenses: axiology, coverage, evolution, logic, semantics, structure
  - issue stance disagreement: 0/6
  - disagreeing stance lenses: none
  - not applicable lenses: none
  - insufficient evidence lenses: none
  - resolution accepted by: 6/6 deliberation participants
  - accepted lenses: axiology, coverage, evolution, semantics, structure, logic
  - remaining disagreement: 0/6 deliberation participants
  - remaining disagreement lenses: none
  - raised by lenses: axiology, coverage
  - issue statement: Shared-root issue across finding-001, finding-006: Inventory allocation depends on non-temporal current/nightly quantity surfaces rather than a time-scoped, source-authoritative availability model. Source finding context: InventoryRecord / InventoryAggregate inventory authority and allocation timing Source finding context: Target: .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:81, .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:86, .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:111, .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:115. Value authority anchor: .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/review-value-alignment-criteria.yaml:6, statement requires suitability as a WMS/TMS/ERP integration concept standard centered on authority, temporality, units, status models, and logistics operating risk. Source finding context: Nightly ERP-aligned inventory snapshots are misaligned with the ontology's declared purpose as an integration concept standard for inventory authority and time-sensitive allocation. Source finding context: For an integration reference document whose review purpose explicitly centers authority, temporality, and logistics operating risk, making intraday allocation depend on a nightly aggregate accepts a hidden tradeoff: accounting reconciliation convenience overrides operational stock truth at the moment orders are accepted. This weakens the ontology's ability to define which system is authoritative for allocation-time inventory and when a quantity is valid. Source finding context: Separate physical, accounting, and allocatable inventory concepts; add as_of/effective_at timestamps; define the authority for allocation-time availability, and require allocations to reference a current WMS-backed or event-sourced availability record rather than a daily ERP-aligned snapshot. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/axiology.findings.yaml#axiology-candidate-001 Source finding context: Use the ontology as the WMS/TMS/ERP integration concept baseline for order, inventory, shipment, and delivery concepts, especially authority and temporality. Source finding context: During intraday order allocation, the ontology directs consumers to a daily InventoryAggregate snapshot while operational WMS and accounting ERP quantities may diverge until nightly reconciliation. Source finding context: The reference model can drive oversell, under-allocation, and audit disputes because it does not identify the valid inventory authority or validity time for allocation decisions. Source finding context: The ontology optimizes for nightly ERP reconciliation as the inventory integration authority without modeling allocation-time validity or separating physical, accounting, and allocatable inventory purposes. Source finding context: InventoryRecord.quantity_on_hand is explicitly shared by WMS and ERP, and discrepancies are adjusted to ERP at night. Source finding context: InventoryAggregate.available_qty is a nightly snapshot but is used for intraday allocation. Source finding context: Because the review purpose requires authority and temporality suitability for WMS/TMS/ERP integration, this snapshot allocation rule undermines the declared purpose. Source finding context: InventoryRecord / InventoryAggregate temporal inventory coverage Source finding context: Embedded materialized input: InventoryRecord `quantity_on_hand` note and comment; InventoryAggregate `available_qty` note; integrity rule for allocation Source finding context: Inventory quantities lack effective-time and movement-history coverage despite being used for allocation. Source finding context: Inventory values are time-dependent. The target itself notes that `InventoryRecord` has no `as_of` concept, while allocation reads `InventoryAggregate.available_qty` even for intraday orders although that aggregate is a nightly snapshot. This leaves a coverage gap for historical reconstruction, oversell investigation, reservation accounting, and WMS/ERP reconciliation. Source finding context: Introduce temporal inventory concepts: `InventorySnapshot` with `as_of`, source system, and scope; `InventoryMovement` or `InventoryTransaction` for receipts, picks, shipments, returns, adjustments; and `Reservation`/`Allocation` with effective time and release/consume events. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-002 Source finding context: Inventory and order fulfillment authority for WMS/TMS/ERP integration. Source finding context: When an order is accepted or audited at a prior point in time, the ontology cannot reconstruct available or on-hand inventory from effective timestamps or movement history. Source finding context: The concept baseline cannot support allocation correctness, dispute analysis, or cross-system reconciliation because only current/nightly quantity surfaces are modeled. Source finding context: Inventory is represented as current/nighly aggregate values without temporal source concepts or movement events. Source finding context: `InventoryRecord.quantity_on_hand` is a current quantity with no effective timestamp. Source finding context: `InventoryAggregate.available_qty` is only a nightly snapshot but is used for intraday allocation decisions. Source finding context: The model lacks inventory movement, reservation, and adjustment concepts that would cover temporal reconstruction and reconciliation.
  - affected purpose: Use the ontology as the WMS/TMS/ERP integration concept baseline for order, inventory, shipment, and delivery concepts, especially authority and temporality. / Inventory and order fulfillment authority for WMS/TMS/ERP integration.
  - failure condition: During intraday order allocation, the ontology directs consumers to a daily InventoryAggregate snapshot while operational WMS and accounting ERP quantities may diverge until nightly reconciliation. / When an order is accepted or audited at a prior point in time, the ontology cannot reconstruct available or on-hand inventory from effective timestamps or movement history.
  - impact: The reference model can drive oversell, under-allocation, and audit disputes because it does not identify the valid inventory authority or validity time for allocation decisions. / The concept baseline cannot support allocation correctness, dispute analysis, or cross-system reconciliation because only current/nightly quantity surfaces are modeled.
  - root hypothesis: Inventory allocation depends on non-temporal current/nightly quantity surfaces rather than a time-scoped, source-authoritative availability model.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/axiology.findings.yaml#axiology-candidate-001`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/review-value-alignment-criteria.yaml:6`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:81`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:86`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:111`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:115`, `Target: .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:81, .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:86, .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:111, .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:115. InventoryAggregate `available_qty` note; integrity rule for allocation`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-001`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-006`, `rel-004`, `finding-relation-graph.yaml#rel-004`, `finding-001.cause-001`, `finding-001.cause-002`, `finding-001.cause-003`, `finding-006.cause-001`, `Embedded materialized input: `InventoryRecord.quantity_on_hand` and adjacent comment`, `finding-006.cause-002`, `Embedded materialized input: `InventoryAggregate.available_qty` note`, `Embedded materialized input: first integrity rule`, `finding-006.cause-003`, `Embedded materialized input: entities list contains InventoryRecord and InventoryAggregate but no movement/reservation/adjustment entity`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-001.axiology`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-001`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-001.coverage`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-001.evolution`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/issue-ledger.yaml#issue-001`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-004`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-001.logic`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/finding-relation-graph.yaml#rel-004`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-001.semantics`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-001`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-001.structure`, `issue-ledger.yaml#dep-001`, `rel-006`, `rel-008`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: axiology, coverage
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - materiality: The declared purpose depends on clear authority and temporality for order, inventory, shipment, and delivery concepts. Using a nightly InventoryAggregate for intraday allocation while WMS operational quantities and ERP accounting quantities may diverge weakens that purpose because consumers cannot know which inventory fact is authoritative, when it is valid, or how to reconstruct allocation decisions later. This can drive oversell, under-allocation, reconciliation failures, and audit disputes.
  - root cause: The starting point is that inventory availability is modeled as shared current or nightly quantity values rather than as source-governed, time-valid inventory assertions. Because InventoryRecord and InventoryAggregate collapse physical, accounting, and allocatable meanings, downstream allocation has no authoritative allocation-time availability record to reference.
  - causal path: The causal path is supported by both surface findings: InventoryRecord.quantity_on_hand is shared between WMS and ERP and reconciled nightly, InventoryAggregate.available_qty is a nightly snapshot, and allocation nevertheless reads that aggregate for intraday decisions. Coverage adds that the model lacks effective-time inventory snapshots, movements, reservations, and adjustments, so prior availability cannot be reconstructed for order acceptance, dispute analysis, or WMS/ERP reconciliation.
  - action: Fix this before the next integration-baseline stage by separating physical, accounting, and allocatable inventory concepts; adding as_of/effective_at validity; defining the source authority for each consuming context; and requiring allocations or reservations to reference a current WMS-backed or event-sourced availability record rather than a daily ERP-aligned snapshot. Add temporal InventorySnapshot, InventoryMovement or InventoryTransaction, and Reservation/Allocation concepts so allocation correctness and audit reconstruction have explicit authority and time semantics.

- issue-005 (high): The ontology has a material fulfillment coverage gap: fulfillment is modeled only from Shipment to Order, so it cannot represent which OrderLine quantity was allocated, picked, shipped, canceled, or backordered in a specific shipment or delivery leg.
  - issue stance agreement: 6/6
  - agreed or narrowed lenses: axiology, coverage, evolution, logic, semantics, structure
  - issue stance disagreement: 0/6
  - disagreeing stance lenses: none
  - not applicable lenses: none
  - insufficient evidence lenses: none
  - resolution accepted by: 2/2 deliberation participants
  - accepted lenses: coverage, axiology
  - remaining disagreement: 1/2 deliberation participants
  - remaining disagreement lenses: axiology
  - raised by lenses: coverage
  - issue statement: OrderLine-level fulfillment allocation is missing, so partial shipment coverage is incomplete. Source finding context: OrderLine / Shipment fulfillment coverage Source finding context: Embedded materialized input: OrderLine attributes comment; relations `fulfills` note Source finding context: For WMS/TMS/ERP integration, fulfillment is not only an order-level state; line quantities may be split, backordered, substituted, or shipped in multiple consignments. Without a line-level fulfillment/allocation concept, the model cannot reconstruct which SKU quantity left in which shipment, weakening reconciliation between order promise, inventory decrement, shipment confirmation, and customer delivery. Source finding context: Add a line-fulfillment concept such as `FulfillmentAllocation` or `ShipmentLine` with refs to `OrderLine`, `Shipment` or `DeliveryLeg`, fulfilled quantity, allocation/pick/ship timestamps, and lifecycle/status for partial, canceled, or backordered quantities. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-001 Source finding context: WMS/TMS/ERP integration concept baseline for order, inventory, shipment, and delivery concepts. Source finding context: When one order has multiple lines or a line is partially fulfilled across multiple shipments, the ontology has no concept to bind the fulfilled quantity to the actual shipment or leg. Source finding context: Operational reconciliation and exception handling become non-actionable because order status, inventory movement, and shipment evidence cannot be traced at the unit where fulfillment actually occurs. Source finding context: The ontology's fulfillment relation is scoped to `Shipment -> Order` and does not include a line-level fulfillment/allocation entity. Source finding context: `OrderLine` has only `sku_ref` and `qty`, with no fulfilled quantity, shipment reference, or delivery-leg reference. Source finding context: The only fulfillment relation is `Shipment fulfills Order`, and its note explicitly excludes OrderLine-level mapping. Source finding context: This prevents the ontology from covering partial shipment and line-level reconciliation scenarios required by the declared integration purpose.
  - affected purpose: WMS/TMS/ERP integration concept baseline for order, inventory, shipment, and delivery concepts.
  - failure condition: When one order has multiple lines or a line is partially fulfilled across multiple shipments, the ontology has no concept to bind the fulfilled quantity to the actual shipment or leg.
  - impact: Operational reconciliation and exception handling become non-actionable because order status, inventory movement, and shipment evidence cannot be traced at the unit where fulfillment actually occurs.
  - root hypothesis: The ontology's fulfillment relation is scoped to `Shipment -> Order` and does not include a line-level fulfillment/allocation entity.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-001`, `OrderLine comment: no reference to actual cargo/segment fulfilling the line`, `relations `fulfills`: Shipment to Order only; note says no OrderLine-level mapping`, `Embedded materialized input: OrderLine attributes comment; relations `fulfills` note`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-005`, `finding-005.cause-001`, `Embedded materialized input: `OrderLine.attributes``, `finding-005.cause-002`, `Embedded materialized input: `relations` entry `fulfills``, `finding-005.cause-003`, `Embedded materialized input header: WMS/TMS/ERP integration concept baseline`, `Embedded materialized input: OrderLine omission comment`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-005.axiology`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-005`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-005.coverage`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-005.evolution`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/issue-ledger.yaml#issue-005`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-001`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-005.logic`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-005.semantics`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-005.structure`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: coverage
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, accept_risk
  - materiality: The declared purpose is a WMS/TMS/ERP integration baseline across order, inventory, shipment, and delivery concepts. That purpose requires reconciliation at the operational unit where fulfillment actually happens: ordered SKU quantities. If a multi-line order or partially fulfilled line is split across shipments, the model cannot bind fulfilled quantity to shipment evidence, inventory decrement, delivery progress, or customer-facing status, making exception handling non-actionable.
  - root cause: The starting point is the ontology’s fulfillment scope: it provides Shipment -> Order fulfillment but lacks a line-level allocation entity that binds an OrderLine quantity to a Shipment or DeliveryLeg. Because the missing concept is upstream of fulfilled quantity, shipment reference, and lifecycle/status details, downstream order or shipment status cannot recover the line-level facts after the fact.
  - causal path: The causal path is accepted by the reviewing lenses: OrderLine only carries sku_ref and qty, the fulfills relation connects Shipment to Order and explicitly omits OrderLine-level mapping, and therefore partial shipments, backorders, substitutions, or split consignments cannot be reconstructed. This directly produces the observed issue: shipment coverage is incomplete for line-level fulfillment scenarios.
  - action: Add a line-fulfillment concept, such as FulfillmentAllocation or ShipmentLine, before advancing the ontology. It should reference OrderLine and Shipment or DeliveryLeg, carry fulfilled quantity, allocation/pick/ship timestamps, and lifecycle/status for partial, canceled, and backordered quantities. This is required to make order promise, inventory movement, shipment confirmation, and delivery evidence traceable through the same fulfillment unit.
  - unresolved disagreement: Deliberation narrowed but did not fully resolve the issue: coverage keeps the issue distinct and high severity because it blocks operational reconstruction, while axiology accepts the issue and action but preserves a medium severity caveat from the value-alignment lens and its relationship to issue-004.

  - affected purpose: 주문 -> 재고 할당 -> 출하 -> 배송의 WMS/TMS/ERP 통합 개념 기준 문서
  - failure condition: 부분출하, 다중창고 출고, 백오더처럼 한 주문 또는 한 주문줄이 여러 화물로 나뉘거나 여러 주문줄이 한 화물에 섞이는 경우
  - impact: 주문줄과 화물의 추적 연속성이 없어 통합 시스템이 어느 재고 할당과 출하가 어떤 고객 주문 품목을 충족했는지 공통 기준으로 판단할 수 없습니다.
  - root hypothesis: 충족 관계의 권위 단위가 OrderLine 수량이 아니라 Order 전체로 고정되어 있다.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-001`, `materialized-input.md:25-31`, `materialized-input.md:105-109`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:25-31,105-109`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-010`, `finding-010.cause-001`, `finding-010.cause-002`, `finding-010.cause-003`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-007.axiology`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-007`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-007.coverage`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-001`, `OrderLine comment: no reference to actual cargo/segment fulfilling the line`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-007.evolution`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/issue-ledger.yaml#issue-007`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-007.logic`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-007.semantics`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-007.structure`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: evolution
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, accept_risk
  - materiality: 검토 대상의 목적은 주문, 재고 할당, 출하, 배송을 WMS/TMS/ERP 통합 기준으로 연결하는 것입니다. 그러나 현재 모델은 Shipment가 Order 전체를 fulfills하는 관계만 두고 OrderLine 수량이 어떤 화물 또는 배송 구간으로 충족됐는지 표현하지 못합니다. 그 결과 한 주문줄이 여러 출하로 나뉘거나 여러 주문줄이 한 화물에 섞이는 경우, 통합 시스템이 어느 재고 할당과 출하가 어느 고객 주문 품목을 충족했는지 공통 기준으로 판단할 수 없어 목적을 약화합니다.
  - root cause: 문제의 출발점은 충족 관계의 권위 단위가 OrderLine 수량이 아니라 Order 전체로 고정되어 있다는 점입니다. OrderLine에는 sku_ref와 qty만 있고 실제 충족 화물이나 배송 구간으로 가는 참조가 없으며, fulfills 관계도 Shipment에서 Order로만 연결됩니다. 따라서 세부 충족 상태를 원천으로 삼아 Order.fulfillment_status를 파생할 구조가 없습니다.
  - causal path: 먼저 OrderLine이 실제 출하 또는 배송 구간과 연결되지 않기 때문에 품목 수량 단위의 충족 추적이 끊깁니다. 이어서 fulfills 관계가 Shipment에서 Order로만 정의되어 OrderLine 단위 매핑을 배제하므로, 부분 수량이 어떤 Shipment나 DeliveryLeg로 이동했는지 표현할 권위 지점이 없습니다. 이 구조에서는 부분출하나 분할충족을 추가할 때 단순히 새 관계를 옆에 붙이는 것으로는 부족하고, 기존 Order-Shipment 충족 의미와 상태 집계 방식을 재정의해야 합니다.
  - action: 릴리스 전 수정하려면 OrderLine 또는 별도 FulfillmentAllocation/ShipmentLine 개념을 도입해 OrderLine 수량의 어느 부분이 어떤 Shipment 또는 DeliveryLeg로 충족되는지 표현해야 합니다. 또한 Order.fulfillment_status는 이 세부 충족 상태에서 파생되도록 권위 관계를 정리해야 합니다. 이 결정은 부분출하, 다중창고 출고, 백오더 확장 전에 닫혀야 하는 구조적 선행 조건입니다.
  - unresolved disagreement: 진화 관점은 확장 차단 효과 때문에 high severity를 유지하지만, axiology 관점은 동일한 root cause를 수용하면서도 가치 목적만 기준으로 보면 medium severity라는 caveat를 남겼습니다.

- issue-010 (high): Inventory quantity and ETA cannot be trusted as integration baseline values until the ontology models which source governs each value, what context it governs, and what evidence explains overrides or supersession.
  - issue stance agreement: 6/6
  - agreed or narrowed lenses: axiology, coverage, evolution, logic, semantics, structure
  - issue stance disagreement: 0/6
  - disagreeing stance lenses: none
  - not applicable lenses: none
  - insufficient evidence lenses: none
  - resolution accepted by: 2/2 deliberation participants
  - accepted lenses: semantics, coverage
  - remaining disagreement: 0/2 deliberation participants
  - remaining disagreement lenses: none
  - raised by lenses: semantics, coverage
  - issue statement: Inventory quantity and ETA authority are left ambiguous because values from WMS, ERP, carriers, and manual operations are not tied to source, governing context, actor, reason, or superseded value. Source finding context: entities.InventoryRecord.attributes.quantity_on_hand Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md: InventoryRecord.quantity_on_hand note "현재 보유 수량. WMS와 ERP가 각자 값을 가지며 야간 배치로 ERP 기준에 맞춘다."; notes "재고 진실: WMS가 창고 실물 기준, ERP가 회계 수량 기준." Source finding context: quantity_on_hand가 물리 재고와 회계 재고라는 서로 다른 의미를 같은 속성명으로 합치고 있다. Source finding context: WMS/TMS/ERP 통합의 기준 문서에서 동일한 quantity_on_hand가 물리적 보유 수량인지, 회계 기준 수량인지, 조정 후 ERP 기준 값인지 구분되지 않으면 동일 필드가 시스템마다 다른 뜻으로 해석된다. 이는 재고 할당, 회계 조정, 운영 조회가 같은 개념을 공유한다는 전제를 약화한다. Source finding context: InventoryRecord의 재고 수량 개념을 권위와 의미별로 분리한다. 예: WMS 권위의 physical_on_hand, ERP 권위의 book_on_hand/accounting_on_hand, reconciliation_delta 또는 reconciliation_status를 별도 속성으로 두고 각 값에 source_system과 as_of/effective_at을 붙인다. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-001 Source finding context: WMS/TMS/ERP 통합의 개념 기준 문서로서 재고 권위와 주문 할당 의미를 일관되게 제공하는 목적. Source finding context: WMS 실물 재고와 ERP 회계 재고가 불일치하거나 야간 배치 전후 값을 동시에 다루는 경우 quantity_on_hand의 의미가 시스템별로 달라진다. Source finding context: 통합 참여자가 같은 속성명을 서로 다른 재고 사실로 매핑할 수 있어 주문 할당 가능성, 회계 재고, 조정 대상 수량이 혼동된다. Source finding context: 재고 수량의 권위 체계가 WMS 물리 기준과 ERP 회계 기준으로 분리되어 있는데 온톨로지는 이를 하나의 quantity_on_hand 의미로 명명한다. Source finding context: quantity_on_hand는 '현재 보유 수량'으로 이름 붙어 있지만 WMS와 ERP가 각자 값을 가진다고 설명된다. Source finding context: WMS 값은 창고 실물 기준이고 ERP 값은 회계 수량 기준이라고 별도 의미가 명시된다. Source finding context: 서로 다른 의미의 재고 수량이 하나의 속성명으로 통합되어 통합 기준 문서의 의미 정합성을 약화한다. Source finding context: Cross-system quantity and ETA authority coverage Source finding context: Embedded materialized input: InventoryRecord `quantity_on_hand` note; notes `재고 진실`; Shipment `eta` note; notes `ETA` Source finding context: Parallel WMS/ERP and carrier/manual values are described without source-of-truth or provenance concepts. Source finding context: Coverage lens treats single authority and audit evidence as required dimensions when values are managed across systems or manually controlled. Here, both inventory and ETA are explicitly multi-source. Without authority/provenance concepts, downstream integrations cannot know which value governs allocation, accounting, display, or audit, and manual changes cannot be explained after the fact. Source finding context: Add source/provenance and authority concepts, for example `SystemOfRecordPolicy`, `QuantityAssertion`, `EtaEstimate`, and `ManualOverride` with source system, effective time, actor, reason, superseded value, and consuming context. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-003 Source finding context: WMS/TMS/ERP integration concept baseline with clear authority for order, inventory, shipment, and delivery values. Source finding context: When WMS and ERP disagree on inventory, or carrier ETA and manual ETA differ, the ontology lacks a concept that identifies the governing value and its evidence. Source finding context: The integration baseline leaves precedence and audit behavior to implementation-specific interpretation, undermining trust in allocation, accounting reconciliation, and customer-facing ETA decisions. Source finding context: The ontology describes multi-source managed values but does not include authority, provenance, or override evidence entities. Source finding context: Inventory quantity is maintained separately by WMS and ERP with different meanings. Source finding context: ETA combines carrier API input and manual operational judgment. Source finding context: No entity or attribute records source-of-truth policy, actor, time, reason, or superseded value for these controlled changes.
  - affected purpose: Use the ontology as a WMS/TMS/ERP integration concept baseline with clear authority for inventory, shipment, ETA, and audit-relevant values. Source finding context: WMS/TMS/ERP 통합의 개념 기준 문서로서 재고 권위와 주문 할당 의미를 일관되게 제공하는 목적. Source finding context: WMS/TMS/ERP integration concept baseline with clear authority for order, inventory, shipment, and delivery values.
  - failure condition: When WMS and ERP disagree on inventory, or carrier ETA and manual ETA differ, consumers need to determine the governing value and its evidence from the ontology. Source finding context: WMS 실물 재고와 ERP 회계 재고가 불일치하거나 야간 배치 전후 값을 동시에 다루는 경우 quantity_on_hand의 의미가 시스템별로 달라진다. Source finding context: When WMS and ERP disagree on inventory, or carrier ETA and manual ETA differ, the ontology lacks a concept that identifies the governing value and its evidence.
  - impact: Precedence and audit behavior are left to implementation-specific interpretation, weakening trust in allocation, accounting reconciliation, and customer-facing ETA decisions. Source finding context: 통합 참여자가 같은 속성명을 서로 다른 재고 사실로 매핑할 수 있어 주문 할당 가능성, 회계 재고, 조정 대상 수량이 혼동된다. Source finding context: The integration baseline leaves precedence and audit behavior to implementation-specific interpretation, undermining trust in allocation, accounting reconciliation, and customer-facing ETA decisions.
  - root hypothesis: The ontology describes multi-source controlled values without source-of-truth, provenance, or override evidence concepts.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-001`, `materialized-input.md InventoryRecord.quantity_on_hand note`, `materialized-input.md notes 재고 진실`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md: InventoryRecord.quantity_on_hand note "현재 보유 수량. WMS와 ERP가 각자 값을 가지며 야간 배치로 ERP 기준에 맞춘다."; notes "재고 진실: WMS가 창고 실물 기준, ERP가 회계 수량 기준."`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-003`, `InventoryRecord `quantity_on_hand` note: WMS and ERP each have values`, `notes `재고 진실`: WMS physical basis, ERP accounting basis, nightly adjustment`, `Shipment `eta` note: operations manually adjusts ETA`, `notes `ETA`: carrier API and manual values are mixed; final display follows operations judgment`, `Embedded materialized input: InventoryRecord `quantity_on_hand` note; notes `재고 진실`; Shipment `eta` note; notes `ETA``, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-015`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-007`, `rel-007`, `finding-relation-graph.yaml#rel-007`, `finding-007.cause-001`, `Embedded materialized input: `InventoryRecord.quantity_on_hand` note`, `Embedded materialized input: notes `재고 진실``, `finding-007.cause-002`, `Embedded materialized input: `Shipment.eta` note`, `Embedded materialized input: notes `ETA``, `finding-007.cause-003`, `Embedded materialized input: entities list includes Shipment, TrackingEvent, InventoryRecord, InventoryAggregate, Carrier, but no authority/provenance/override entity`, `finding-015.cause-001`, `finding-015.cause-002`, `finding-015.cause-003`, `materialized-input.md InventoryRecord definition`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-010.axiology`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-010`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-010.coverage`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-010.evolution`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/issue-ledger.yaml#issue-010`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-004`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-010.logic`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/finding-relation-graph.yaml#rel-007`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-010.semantics`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-010.structure`, `issue-ledger.yaml#dep-001`, `rel-006`, `rel-008`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: semantics, coverage
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - materiality: The stated purpose is to provide a WMS/TMS/ERP concept baseline with clear authority for inventory, shipment, ETA, and audit-relevant values. That purpose is weakened because WMS physical inventory, ERP accounting inventory, carrier ETA, and manually adjusted ETA are all described as possible values, but the ontology does not say which value controls allocation, accounting reconciliation, customer display, or audit explanation when they differ.
  - root cause: The causal chain starts with the ontology treating multi-source controlled values as ordinary attributes instead of modeling source-of-truth, provenance, and override evidence. Because the authority layer is absent, downstream consumers receive values without governing source, consuming context, actor, reason, time, or superseded value.
  - causal path: The evidence path combines two supported findings: InventoryRecord.quantity_on_hand merges WMS physical quantity and ERP accounting quantity under one attribute, while Shipment.eta mixes carrier API values with manual operations judgment. Since no authority, provenance, or override entity records the governing policy and change evidence, precedence is left to each implementation, creating inconsistent interpretations for allocation, accounting, ETA display, and audit.
  - action: Fix this before the next stage by adding explicit authority and provenance concepts such as SystemOfRecordPolicy, QuantityAssertion, EtaEstimate, and ManualOverride. These should capture source system, effective/as-of time, governing consuming context, actor, reason, superseded value, and reconciliation or override status so inventory and ETA consumers can determine the authoritative value and explain why it governs.

- issue-014 (high): OrderLine fulfillment is materially incomplete because the ontology describes shipment composition in terms of order lines but provides no structural way to say which OrderLine quantity was fulfilled by which Shipment or DeliveryLeg.
  - issue stance agreement: 6/6
  - agreed or narrowed lenses: axiology, coverage, evolution, logic, semantics, structure
  - issue stance disagreement: 0/6
  - disagreeing stance lenses: none
  - not applicable lenses: none
  - insufficient evidence lenses: none
  - resolution accepted by: 2/2 deliberation participants
  - accepted lenses: structure, axiology
  - remaining disagreement: 1/2 deliberation participants
  - remaining disagreement lenses: axiology
  - raised by lenses: structure
  - issue statement: OrderLine fulfillment is structurally disconnected from Shipment and DeliveryLeg. Source finding context: logistics-fulfillment-ontology.yaml / OrderLine- Shipment 연결 구조 Source finding context: materialized-input.md: OrderLine attributes only include sku_ref and qty; comment states no reference from the line to the actual fulfilling shipment/leg. relations defines only fulfills from Shipment to Order with note that OrderLine-level mapping is not included. Source finding context: The target’s own comments identify partial-shipment traceability as impossible, and Shipment.total_weight is defined as the sum of constituent OrderLine weights while no structural edge identifies those constituent lines. For a WMS/TMS/ERP integration reference, this weakens the structural basis for reconciling line-level allocation, picking, shipment contents, and delivery evidence. Source finding context: Add a line-level fulfillment/allocation structure, such as ShipmentLine or FulfillmentAllocation, linking OrderLine to Shipment and optionally DeliveryLeg, with shipped/allocated quantity and status-relevant timestamps. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/structure.findings.yaml#structure-candidate-001 Source finding context: Concept reference document for WMS/TMS/ERP integration across order, inventory, shipment, and delivery concepts. Source finding context: When one order is split across multiple shipments or one shipment contains selected lines from multiple orders, the ontology cannot identify which OrderLine quantity was fulfilled by which physical movement. Source finding context: Integration consumers cannot reliably reconcile OMS line status, WMS pick/pack records, TMS shipment contents, and carrier delivery events at the line level, reducing the ontology’s usefulness as a concept standard. Source finding context: The fulfillment relation is modeled only at order granularity even though shipment composition is described in terms of order lines. Source finding context: OrderLine has no attribute or relation to Shipment or DeliveryLeg. Source finding context: The only explicit fulfillment edge connects Shipment to Order, not Shipment to OrderLine. Source finding context: Shipment composition nevertheless depends on OrderLine membership.
  - affected purpose: Concept reference document for WMS/TMS/ERP integration across order, inventory, shipment, and delivery concepts.
  - failure condition: When one order is split across multiple shipments or one shipment contains selected lines from multiple orders, the ontology cannot identify which OrderLine quantity was fulfilled by which physical movement.
  - impact: Integration consumers cannot reliably reconcile OMS line status, WMS pick/pack records, TMS shipment contents, and carrier delivery events at the line level, reducing the ontology’s usefulness as a concept standard.
  - root hypothesis: The fulfillment relation is modeled only at order granularity even though shipment composition is described in terms of order lines.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/structure.findings.yaml#structure-candidate-001`, `materialized-input.md: OrderLine has sku_ref and qty only, plus an explicit comment that no reference exists to the shipment/leg that fulfilled the line.`, `materialized-input.md: relations contains only fulfills from Shipment to Order and notes that OrderLine-level mapping is omitted.`, `materialized-input.md: Shipment.total_weight note says it is the sum of constituent OrderLine weights.`, `materialized-input.md: OrderLine attributes only include sku_ref and qty; comment states no reference from the line to the actual fulfilling shipment/leg. relations defines only fulfills from Shipment to Order with note that OrderLine-level mapping is not included.`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-019`, `finding-019.cause-001`, `materialized-input.md: OrderLine attributes list only sku_ref and qty.`, `materialized-input.md: OrderLine comment says there is no reference to the actual shipment/leg that fulfilled the line.`, `finding-019.cause-002`, `materialized-input.md: relations entry name fulfills, from Shipment, to Order.`, `materialized-input.md: fulfills note says it is an order-level relation and does not include OrderLine-level mapping.`, `finding-019.cause-003`, `materialized-input.md: Shipment.total_weight note says it is calculated as the sum of constituent OrderLine weights.`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-014.axiology`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-014`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-014.coverage`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-001`, `OrderLine comment: no reference to actual cargo/segment fulfilling the line`, `relations `fulfills`: Shipment to Order only; note says no OrderLine-level mapping`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-014.evolution`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/issue-ledger.yaml#issue-014`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-001`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-014.logic`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-014.semantics`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-014.structure`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: structure
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, accept_risk
  - materiality: The declared purpose is a WMS/TMS/ERP integration reference across order, inventory, shipment, and delivery concepts. Without line-level fulfillment traceability, consumers cannot reconcile OMS line status, WMS pick/pack records, TMS shipment contents, and carrier delivery events when orders are split, partially shipped, or mixed into shipments with lines from multiple orders.
  - root cause: The causal chain starts with fulfillment being modeled only at order granularity. Shipment can fulfill Order, but the ontology omits the allocation concept needed to connect specific OrderLine quantities to the physical shipment or leg that moved them, even though shipment composition depends on those lines.
  - causal path: OrderLine only carries sku_ref and qty and has no relation to Shipment or DeliveryLeg. The explicit fulfills relation connects Shipment to Order and is documented as excluding OrderLine-level mapping. At the same time, Shipment.total_weight is defined from constituent OrderLine weights, so the model depends on line membership while leaving that membership structurally unstated.
  - action: Add a line-level fulfillment or allocation structure, such as ShipmentLine or FulfillmentAllocation, that links OrderLine to Shipment and optionally DeliveryLeg, with shipped or allocated quantity and status-relevant timestamps. This should be closed before the next stage because downstream reconciliation, shipment contents, partial fulfillment, and delivery evidence depend on this mapping.
  - unresolved disagreement: Deliberation accepted the root and narrowed the issue to a structural line-level traceability defect, but preserved axiology's caveat that severity is medium under value-purpose criteria while structure keeps high severity for structural completeness.

- issue-002 (medium): Freight weights, dimensions, and shipment totals are not safe integration values while they remain bare numbers or strings. The ontology must model them as structured measurements with explicit value, unit, source, conversion basis, and relevant package or handling-unit level before it can serve as a unit-consistent WMS/TMS/ERP baseline.
  - issue stance agreement: 6/6
  - agreed or narrowed lenses: axiology, coverage, evolution, logic, semantics, structure
  - issue stance disagreement: 0/6

  - not applicable lenses: none
  - insufficient evidence lenses: none
  - resolution accepted by: 2/2 deliberation participants
  - accepted lenses: axiology, coverage
  - remaining disagreement: 0/2 deliberation participants
  - remaining disagreement lenses: none

  - issue statement: Shared-root issue across finding-002, finding-009: Freight measurement failures stem from representing logistics measurements as bare numbers or strings instead of structured value/unit/source measurements.   - materiality: This is material because the declared purpose depends on unit consistency and logistics operating risk control. If SKU measurements from different warehouses or source systems can be combined without known units or basis, shipment totals can be numerically valid-looking but operationally wrong, weakening carrier rating, routing, capacity planning, cost calculation, reconciliation, and trust in the ontology.
  - root cause: The starting point is the ontology's choice to represent measurements as source-dependent bare values instead of a canonical Measurement concept. Once unit, source, precision, conversion policy, and package level are absent at the SKU and dimension fields, downstream shipment calculations have no authoritative semantics to aggregate or compare.
  - causal path: The causal chain begins with Sku.weight and Sku.dims allowing warehouse-specific or source-system-dependent units. Shipment.total_weight then sums line weights and stores the result without unit, derivation source, timestamp, package hierarchy, or dimensional-weight basis. That propagates local measurement ambiguity into shipment-level values used by freight and warehouse integrations.
  - action: Fix this before the next stage by introducing normalized measurement concepts for weight and dimensions, including value, unit of measure, precision, source, effective time, and conversion policy. Define canonical integration units for shipment totals, store total_weight with unit and derivation metadata, and attach gross, net, dimensional weight, and dimensions to the correct package or handling-unit level such as carton, pallet, parcel, or shipment container.

  - source lenses: axiology, evolution
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - materiality: This weakens the declared purpose of serving as the WMS/TMS/ERP integration status-model and authority baseline. If carrier event meanings and shipment status transitions are left to each integration, consumers can disagree on in_transit, exception, delivered, and other terminal or non-happy-path states, undermining coordination, customer notifications, exception handling, and auditability.
  - root cause: The starting point is the absence of a versioned canonical StatusMapping or EventNormalization concept between external carrier codes and internal shipment states. Without that authority layer, status meaning is owned by adapters and fixed enums rather than by the ontology's shared integration model.
  - causal path: Shipment.status and TrackingEvent.event_type are modeled as separate fixed vocabularies. Carrier-status mapping is then delegated to individual integrations, while OMS, TMS, and carrier status fields are managed independently. That structure allows inconsistent internal state derivation today and makes future carrier codes, exceptions, returns, re-delivery, lost shipment, or correction states require enum changes or integration-by-integration reinterpretation.
  - action: Define a canonical lifecycle/status mapping table before treating the ontology as the shared status baseline. The table should separate external carrier_event_code from canonical shipment state, include mapping versions, transition rules, terminal-state precedence, exception handling, and the authority allowed to advance or correct each state; stable enums should remain high-level categories while detailed external codes live in extensible mapping/code-set structures.

- issue-004 (medium): Issue-004 is a material traceability gap: the ontology needs a line-level fulfillment/allocation authority, not only a Shipment-to-Order fulfillment relation, to serve as an order, freight, and delivery integration standard.

  - issue statement: Omitting OrderLine-to-shipment fulfillment mapping is misaligned with the ontology's fulfillment integration purpose because it sacrifices operational traceability for a simpler order-level relation. Source finding context: OrderLine fulfillment traceability through Shipment / DeliveryLeg Source finding context: Target: .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:25, .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:30, .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:105, .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:109. Source finding context: A logistics fulfillment concept baseline must support the operational question of which ordered item moved in which shipment, especially for partial shipments and exceptions. The model chooses a simpler order-level relation, but that local simplification weakens the broader purpose of coordinating orders, freight, and delivery across systems. Source finding context: Add a fulfillment allocation/detail concept linking OrderLine to Shipment or DeliveryLeg with shipped quantity, split/partial flags, timestamps, and exception/cancellation state. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/axiology.findings.yaml#axiology-candidate-004 Source finding context: Use the ontology as a concept standard across order, freight, and delivery workflows while controlling logistics operating risk. Source finding context: The reference model cannot explain which ordered quantities were fulfilled by which physical movements, undermining exception resolution, customer service, reconciliation, and auditability. Source finding context: The ontology optimizes for order-level fulfillment simplicity instead of preserving line-level operational traceability. Source finding context: OrderLine contains product and quantity but no fulfillment shipment or leg reference. Source finding context: The only fulfills relation is Shipment to Order and explicitly omits OrderLine-level mapping. Source finding context: Because the declared review purpose is suitability as an order, freight, and delivery integration standard with logistics risk focus, this omission weakens purpose alignment.
  - affected purpose: Use the ontology as a concept standard across order, freight, and delivery workflows while controlling logistics operating risk.
  - failure condition: An order is partially shipped, split across shipments, or has an exception on one line, but the ontology only records a Shipment-to-Order relation.
  - impact: The reference model cannot explain which ordered quantities were fulfilled by which physical movements, undermining exception resolution, customer service, reconciliation, and auditability.
  - root hypothesis: The ontology optimizes for order-level fulfillment simplicity instead of preserving line-level operational traceability.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/axiology.findings.yaml#axiology-candidate-004`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/review-value-alignment-criteria.yaml:6`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:25`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:30`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:105`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:109`, `Target: .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:25, .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:30, .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:105, .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:109. Value authority anchor: .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/review-value-alignment-criteria.yaml:6, statement requires an order/inventory/shipment/delivery concept standard focused on logistics operating risk.`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-004`, `finding-004.cause-001`, `finding-004.cause-002`, `finding-004.cause-003`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-004.axiology`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-004`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-004.coverage`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-001`, `relations `fulfills`: Shipment to Order only; note says no OrderLine-level mapping`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-004.evolution`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/issue-ledger.yaml#issue-004`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-001`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-004.logic`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-004.semantics`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-004.structure`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: axiology
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, accept_risk
  - materiality: The declared purpose is to support order, freight, and delivery workflows while controlling logistics operating risk. When fulfillment is recorded only at order level, the ontology cannot answer which ordered item quantity moved in which shipment or delivery leg, especially in partial shipments, split fulfillment, exceptions, cancellations, reconciliation, customer service, or audit scenarios. That weakens the ontology's operational integration value rather than merely leaving out a convenience detail.
  - root cause: The accepted root is the absence of a line-level fulfillment/allocation concept that links OrderLine quantities to Shipment or DeliveryLeg evidence. This is the starting point because OrderLine carries product and quantity, but fulfillment evidence is attached only to the order as a whole, so all downstream traceability questions lose the line and quantity authority they require.
  - causal path: The causal path is: OrderLine has product and quantity without a shipment or leg reference; the only fulfillment relation links Shipment to Order and explicitly omits OrderLine-level mapping; therefore partial, split, exception, reconciliation, and audit cases collapse into an order-level fulfilled/not-fulfilled view. The lenses converged that this missing allocation/detail structure causes the observed operational traceability failure, while narrowing the framing from a broad simplicity tradeoff to the specific absent line-level authority.
  - action: Add a fulfillment allocation/detail concept linking OrderLine to Shipment or DeliveryLeg, including shipped quantity, split or partial flags, timestamps, and exception or cancellation state. This should be closed before the next stage because later fulfillment, exception, reconciliation, and audit semantics depend on this authority being present rather than inferred from order-level shipment links.
  - unresolved disagreement: All participating lenses accepted the missing line-level allocation concept as the actionable root, but severity remains unresolved: axiology, semantics, and logic keep the medium framing, while coverage, evolution, and structure support raising severity because of reconciliation, exception handling, auditability, and future fulfillment patterns.

- issue-006 (medium): The ontology’s order and shipment lifecycles are materially incomplete because they stop at normal forward progress to delivered while tracking events can already report exceptions. This leaves cancellation, failed delivery, return, reissue, correction, reversal, and archival closure without canonical lifecycle representation.

  - issue statement: Order and Shipment lifecycle coverage omits cancellation, failed delivery, return, reissue, correction, reversal, and archival phases despite TrackingEvent supporting exceptions. Source finding context: Embedded materialized input: Order `fulfillment_status` values; Shipment `status` values and note; TrackingEvent `event_type` values Source finding context: Lifecycle states stop at happy-path delivery and omit cancellation, exception resolution, return, correction, and archival phases. Source finding context: A logistics fulfillment baseline must cover operational endings and post-final corrections. The current state models treat `delivered` as final while still having carrier exceptions and multi-system updates elsewhere, so real operations such as failed delivery, return-to-sender, cancellation after allocation, shipment reissue, proof-of-delivery correction, and audit closure are outside the conceptual lifecycle. Source finding context: Extend lifecycle coverage with explicit terminal and post-terminal concepts: canceled/backordered for orders or lines; exception/failed_delivery/returned/reissued for shipments; correction or reversal events; and archival/audit-closed states with rules for how post-delivery events affect status. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-004 Source finding context: Order, freight, and delivery concept consistency for logistics operations. Source finding context: When fulfillment leaves the happy path or a delivered shipment must be corrected, the ontology has no lifecycle concept to represent the condition without overloading existing states. Source finding context: Operations risk is hidden because exceptions and reversals cannot be represented consistently across OMS, TMS, carrier tracking, and ERP reconciliation. Source finding context: Lifecycle enumerations are limited to forward-progress delivery states and define delivered as final. Source finding context: Order and shipment statuses enumerate only normal forward progress to delivered. Source finding context: Carrier tracking can report `exception`, but shipment status has no exception or recovery state. Source finding context: The ontology therefore does not cover cancellation, failed delivery, returns, reissue, correction, or archival lifecycle phases.
  - affected purpose: Order, freight, and delivery concept consistency for logistics operations and WMS/TMS/ERP reconciliation. Source finding context: Order, freight, and delivery concept consistency for logistics operations.
  - failure condition: Fulfillment leaves the happy path, a delivered shipment must be corrected, or cancellation/return/reissue needs to be represented without overloading existing statuses. Source finding context: When fulfillment leaves the happy path or a delivered shipment must be corrected, the ontology has no lifecycle concept to represent the condition without overloading existing states.
  - impact: Exceptions and reversals cannot be represented consistently across OMS, TMS, carrier tracking, and ERP reconciliation, hiding operational risk. Source finding context: Operations risk is hidden because exceptions and reversals cannot be represented consistently across OMS, TMS, carrier tracking, and ERP reconciliation.
  - root hypothesis: Lifecycle enumerations are limited to forward-progress delivery states and define delivered as final, leaving non-happy-path and post-terminal logistics states outside the model.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-004`, `Order `fulfillment_status` values: received, allocated, picking, shipped, delivered`, `Shipment `status` values: created, picked, in_transit, delivered; note says delivered is final`, `TrackingEvent `event_type` includes exception`, `Embedded materialized input: Order `fulfillment_status` values; Shipment `status` values and note; TrackingEvent `event_type` values`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-008`, `finding-008.cause-001`, `Embedded materialized input: `Order.fulfillment_status` values`, `Embedded materialized input: `Shipment.status` values`, `finding-008.cause-002`, `Embedded materialized input: `TrackingEvent.event_type` values`, `finding-008.cause-003`, `Embedded materialized input: entity/status definitions contain no cancellation/return/correction/archive states`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-006.axiology`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-006`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-006.coverage`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-006.evolution`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/issue-ledger.yaml#issue-006`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-003`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-006.logic`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-006.semantics`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-004`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-006.structure`, `issue-ledger.yaml#dep-003`, `rel-016`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/problem-framing.yaml`

  - action candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - materiality: This weakens order, freight, and delivery concept consistency for logistics operations and WMS/TMS/ERP reconciliation because non-happy-path outcomes and post-delivery corrections cannot be represented consistently. When OMS, TMS, carrier tracking, and ERP systems need to coordinate an exception, reversal, or correction, the model forces those conditions into happy-path statuses or leaves them outside the lifecycle, hiding operational risk.
  - root cause: The starting point is the fixed lifecycle enumeration: order and shipment statuses cover only forward movement toward delivery, and shipment delivery is treated as final. Because the lifecycle itself has no terminal, recovery, or post-terminal concepts, downstream handling for exception, return, correction, reissue, or audit closure has no stable status meaning to attach to.
  - causal path: The causal path is straightforward: Order fulfillment statuses run from received through delivered, and Shipment statuses run from created through delivered. TrackingEvent can report an exception, but Shipment has no corresponding exception or recovery state. As a result, the ontology can observe that something went wrong while lacking lifecycle states for cancellation, failed delivery, return, reissue, correction, reversal, or archival closure.
  - action: Extend lifecycle coverage before relying on the ontology as an operational standard. Add explicit non-happy-path, terminal, and post-terminal concepts such as canceled or backordered order states, failed_delivery, returned, reissued, exception or recovery shipment states, correction or reversal events, and archival or audit-closed states, with rules for how exception and correction events affect order, shipment, and audit status.

  - materiality: 선언된 목적은 재고 할당과 WMS/ERP 재고 권위 통합 기준입니다. 현재 모델은 주문 일중에도 야간 InventoryAggregate.available_qty를 읽고, InventoryRecord.quantity_on_hand도 현재 보유 수량 중심이라 주문 당시 재고 판단, 이후 조정, ERP 기준 reconcile 결과를 같은 수량 표면에 섞게 됩니다. 그래서 실시간 원천이나 감사 가능한 재고 이력을 추가하면 기존 주문 판단을 재현하기 어렵고, 새 재고 원천과의 통합 연속성도 약해집니다.
  - root cause: 시작점은 Inventory quantity concepts lack structured effective time and source authority입니다. 수량 자체에 as_of/effective_at 같은 유효 시점과 source_system 같은 권위 원천이 붙어 있지 않으면, WMS 물리 재고, ERP 회계 재고, 야간 스냅샷, 실시간 이벤트가 서로 다른 주장인지 같은 값의 갱신인지 구분할 기준이 없습니다.
  - causal path: InventoryRecord.quantity_on_hand는 WMS와 ERP가 각자 값을 가진 뒤 야간 배치로 ERP 기준에 맞춰지는 현재 수량이고, InventoryAggregate.available_qty는 매일 밤 저장되는 SKU별 전사 가용 재고 스냅샷입니다. 그런데 재고 할당은 일중 주문에서도 이 nightly snapshot 값을 읽습니다. 동시에 as_of 개념 부재가 명시되어 주문 접수 당시 재고 같은 시점 질의가 불가능하므로, 실시간 또는 감사 가능한 재고 체계로 확장할 때 기존 현재값과 야간값 필드의 의미를 재정의해야 하는 causal path가 성립합니다.
  - action: InventoryRecord와 InventoryAggregate에 as_of/effective_at, source_system, snapshot_type, reconciliation_status를 도입하고, 할당은 단순 현재 스냅샷 값이 아니라 특정 시점의 가용성 이벤트 또는 재고 원장 기준을 참조하게 해야 합니다. 이 변경은 다음 단계 통합 전에 닫아야 합니다. 먼저 수량의 시간·원천 권위를 모델의 기준 개념으로 세워야 이후 실시간 WMS, ERP 조정, 주문 시점 감사, 회계 마감 재고가 기존 데이터 의미를 덮어쓰지 않고 연결됩니다.

- issue-009 (medium): issue-009 is a material medium issue: DeliveryLeg is modeled as a subtype of Shipment even though it represents route segments, so route reasoning needs composition and ordered containment under a parent Shipment or Route instead of inheritance.
  - issue stance agreement: 5/6
  - agreed or narrowed lenses: axiology, evolution, logic, semantics, structure
  - issue stance disagreement: 0/6
  - disagreeing stance lenses: none
  - not applicable lenses: none
  - insufficient evidence lenses: coverage
  - resolution accepted by: 6/6 deliberation participants
  - accepted lenses: logic, structure, coverage, axiology, evolution, semantics
  - remaining disagreement: 0/6 deliberation participants
  - remaining disagreement lenses: none
  - raised by lenses: logic, structure
  - issue statement: Shared-root issue across finding-014, finding-020: DeliveryLeg route reasoning is unstable because the ontology uses inheritance where it needs shipment-route composition and ordering. Source finding context: logistics-fulfillment-ontology.yaml / entities.Shipment and entities.DeliveryLeg Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:40, .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:52 Source finding context: DeliveryLeg is declared as a Shipment while its definition allows route segments that are not complete warehouse-to-customer shipments. Source finding context: This is an inter-claim necessary-type conflict: the subtype declaration requires all DeliveryLeg instances to be Shipment instances, while the DeliveryLeg definition permits instances whose origin/destination pattern does not meet the Shipment definition. It matters for WMS/TMS/ERP concept integration because code or mappings that inherit Shipment obligations onto each DeliveryLeg can treat a partial leg as a full customer shipment, weakening status, carrier, and fulfillment reasoning. Source finding context: Make `Shipment` the whole consignment/order movement and model `DeliveryLeg` as a component of Shipment instead of `is_a`, or broaden `Shipment` to mean any physical cargo movement and introduce a separate whole-shipment concept for warehouse-to-customer fulfillment. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/logic.findings.yaml#logic-candidate-001 Source finding context: Concept baseline for WMS/TMS/ERP integration across order, freight, and delivery concepts. Source finding context: When DeliveryLeg instances such as hub-to-customer are interpreted through the `is_a: Shipment` declaration, they must also satisfy the narrower Shipment definition of warehouse-to-customer movement. Source finding context: The ontology can classify partial route legs as full shipments, making downstream integration rules about shipment status, fulfillment, and delivery scope logically unstable. Source finding context: The ontology uses inheritance for a part-of route segment relationship while keeping the parent definition narrower than the child’s allowed instances. Source finding context: `Shipment` is defined as movement from warehouse to customer. Source finding context: `DeliveryLeg` is declared `is_a: Shipment`. Source finding context: `DeliveryLeg` permits route segments such as warehouse-to-hub and hub-to-customer, which are not necessarily full warehouse-to-customer movements. Source finding context: logistics-fulfillment-ontology.yaml / DeliveryLeg 경로 구성 구조 Source finding context: materialized-input.md: Shipment definition says DeliveryLeg is also a kind of Shipment; DeliveryLeg has leg_seq, from_ref, and to_ref; relations contains only fulfills from Shipment to Order and no parent shipment, route, or next-leg relation. Source finding context: DeliveryLeg has no structural link that groups legs into a shipment route. Source finding context: A sequence number is only meaningful within a containing route or shipment. Because DeliveryLeg is modeled only as an is_a Shipment subtype, each leg inherits shipment-level behavior but lacks a structural edge that says which shipment route it belongs to or which legs precede/follow it. Source finding context: Introduce a parent/composition relation such as Shipment has_many DeliveryLeg, DeliveryLeg belongs_to Shipment, or a separate Route entity with ordered legs; scope leg_seq to that parent. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/structure.findings.yaml#structure-candidate-002 Source finding context: Concept reference document for shipment and delivery integration between WMS/TMS/carrier systems. Source finding context: When a delivery has multiple legs, consumers cannot reconstruct the end-to-end route or associate leg-level tracking and locations with one parent shipment. Source finding context: TMS/carrier integration loses a stable structural path for multi-leg delivery state, ETA, and exception handling, even though DeliveryLeg is declared as part of the shipment model. Source finding context: The ontology uses inheritance to relate DeliveryLeg to Shipment but omits the composition or ordering relation required for route-level structure. Source finding context: DeliveryLeg is a route segment with leg_seq/from/to attributes. Source finding context: No explicit relation groups DeliveryLeg instances under a parent shipment or route. Source finding context: The is_a Shipment link makes each leg a kind of shipment but does not provide route containment or adjacency.
  - affected purpose: Concept baseline for WMS/TMS/ERP integration across order, freight, and delivery concepts. / Concept reference document for shipment and delivery integration between WMS/TMS/carrier systems.
  - failure condition: When DeliveryLeg instances such as hub-to-customer are interpreted through the `is_a: Shipment` declaration, they must also satisfy the narrower Shipment definition of warehouse-to-customer movement. / When a delivery has multiple legs, consumers cannot reconstruct the end-to-end route or associate leg-level tracking and locations with one parent shipment.
  - impact: The ontology can classify partial route legs as full shipments, making downstream integration rules about shipment status, fulfillment, and delivery scope logically unstable. / TMS/carrier integration loses a stable structural path for multi-leg delivery state, ETA, and exception handling, even though DeliveryLeg is declared as part of the shipment model.
  - root hypothesis: DeliveryLeg route reasoning is unstable because the ontology uses inheritance where it needs shipment-route composition and ordering.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/logic.findings.yaml#logic-candidate-001`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:40-49`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:52-58`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:40, .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:52`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/structure.findings.yaml#structure-candidate-002`, `materialized-input.md: DeliveryLeg definition says it is one segment of a cargo route.`, `materialized-input.md: DeliveryLeg attributes include leg_seq, from_ref, and to_ref but no parent shipment or route reference.`, `materialized-input.md: relations contains only fulfills from Shipment to Order.`, `materialized-input.md: Shipment definition says DeliveryLeg is also a kind of Shipment; DeliveryLeg has leg_seq, from_ref, and to_ref; relations contains only fulfills from Shipment to Order and no parent shipment, route, or next-leg relation.`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-014`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-020`, `rel-018`, `finding-relation-graph.yaml#rel-018`, `finding-014.cause-001`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:40-41`, `finding-014.cause-002`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:52-54`, `finding-014.cause-003`, `finding-020.cause-001`, `materialized-input.md: DeliveryLeg definition and attributes.`, `finding-020.cause-002`, `materialized-input.md: DeliveryLeg attributes do not include parent shipment or route reference.`, `finding-020.cause-003`, `materialized-input.md: Shipment definition says DeliveryLeg is also a kind of Shipment.`, `materialized-input.md: DeliveryLeg has is_a: Shipment.`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-009.axiology`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-009`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-009.coverage`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-009.evolution`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/issue-ledger.yaml#issue-009`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-009.logic`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/finding-relation-graph.yaml#rel-018`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-009.semantics`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-002`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-009.structure`, `issue-ledger.yaml#dep-004`, `rel-019`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: logic, structure
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - materiality: This weakens the declared WMS/TMS/ERP integration purpose because downstream systems can treat partial legs, such as warehouse-to-hub or hub-to-customer movements, as complete customer shipments. That destabilizes shipment status, fulfillment scope, carrier handoff, ETA, and exception reasoning, and also prevents consumers from reconstructing a multi-leg route under one stable parent shipment.
  - root cause: The starting point is the part-whole modeling error: Shipment is the whole consignment or order movement, while DeliveryLeg is a segment of that movement. Declaring DeliveryLeg as `is_a: Shipment` transfers whole-shipment obligations to each leg, but it does not create the containment or route scope that leg sequencing requires.
  - causal path: The evidence chain is that Shipment is defined as warehouse-to-customer movement, DeliveryLeg is declared `is_a: Shipment`, and DeliveryLeg permits segment endpoints such as warehouse-to-hub and hub-to-customer that are not necessarily full shipments. Structurally, DeliveryLeg has `leg_seq`, `from_ref`, and `to_ref`, but no parent shipment, route, or adjacency relation; therefore sequence has no containing scope and each leg inherits shipment-level meaning without being placed in an end-to-end route.
  - action: Fix the model before the next stage by choosing a clear route authority: either keep Shipment as the whole consignment/order movement and model DeliveryLeg as a contained component of Shipment, or broaden Shipment to any cargo movement and introduce a separate whole-shipment fulfillment concept. In either case, add an explicit parent/composition relation, such as Shipment has_many DeliveryLeg, DeliveryLeg belongs_to Shipment, or a Route entity with ordered legs, and scope `leg_seq` to that parent.

- issue-011 (medium): DeliveryLeg를 Shipment의 하위 유형으로 둔 현재 모델은 배송 구간과 전체 출하를 같은 종류로 취급하므로 수정이 필요하다. DeliveryLeg는 Shipment 자체가 아니라 Shipment 또는 Route에 속한 route segment로 분리되어야 한다.
