  - remaining disagreement: 0/2 deliberation participants
  - remaining disagreement lenses: none
  - raised by lenses: axiology, coverage
  - issue statement: Shared-root issue across finding-002, finding-009: Freight measurement failures stem from representing logistics measurements as bare numbers or strings instead of structured value/unit/source measurements. Source finding context: Sku.weight / Sku.dims and Shipment.total_weight Source finding context: Target: .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:37, .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:38, .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:48. Value authority anchor: .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/review-value-alignment-criteria.yaml:6, statement requires a WMS/TMS/ERP integration concept standard centered on unit consistency and logistics operating risk. Source finding context: Leaving product measurements in source-system units while summing total shipment weight is misaligned with the unit-consistency purpose of the integration ontology. Source finding context: A concept baseline for WMS/TMS/ERP integration should prevent unit ambiguity at integration boundaries. Here, the local convenience of preserving source units is accepted without a canonical unit or conversion model, while downstream shipment total_weight depends on those measurements. That hidden tradeoff can corrupt carrier rating, capacity planning, and exception handling. Source finding context: Represent weight and dimensions as structured measurements with value, unit, source, and conversion policy; define canonical integration units for shipment totals; store total_weight with its unit and derivation timestamp/source. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/axiology.findings.yaml#axiology-candidate-002 Source finding context: Use the ontology as the concept baseline for unit-consistent WMS/TMS/ERP integration and logistics risk control. Source finding context: When SKUs from different warehouses or source systems are combined into shipments, total_weight may sum values recorded in incompatible or unknown units. Source finding context: The model can produce incorrect shipment weight and dimensions, weakening carrier integration, cost calculation, load planning, and operational trust in the reference ontology. Source finding context: The ontology preserves source-system measurement conventions instead of defining a canonical measurement concept for integration use. Source finding context: Sku.weight and Sku.dims allow source-specific or unspecified units. Source finding context: Shipment.total_weight is derived by summing line weights, so unit ambiguity propagates into a shipment-level operational value. Source finding context: Because the declared review purpose prioritizes unit consistency and logistics operating risk, this choice undermines the ontology's intended integration role. Source finding context: Sku measurement and packaging/unit coverage Source finding context: Embedded materialized input: Sku `weight` note; Sku `dims` note; Shipment `total_weight` note Source finding context: Measurement units and package handling are under-modeled for freight and warehouse integration. Source finding context: Weight, dimensions, and packaging drive carrier rating, routing, capacity, customs-adjacent documentation, warehouse handling, and shipment reconciliation. Even though international/customs is out of scope, domestic WMS/TMS integration still needs consistent units and packaging hierarchy to avoid incorrect total weights and carrier handoff ambiguity. Source finding context: Add normalized measurement concepts with unit of measure, precision, source, and effective time; represent package/handling-unit hierarchy such as carton, pallet, parcel, and shipment container; and attach gross/net/dimensional weight and dimensions to the appropriate level. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-005 Source finding context: Freight, warehouse, and TMS integration concept baseline. Source finding context: When SKUs or shipments cross warehouses/carriers using different measurement conventions, the ontology cannot determine the unit, measurement basis, or package level of weight/dimensions. Source finding context: Carrier rating, routing, capacity planning, and reconciliation can be wrong because numeric measurements are not semantically comparable across systems. Source finding context: Measurements are represented as bare numbers/strings without unit or handling-unit concepts. Source finding context: SKU weight is a number whose unit depends on warehouse convention. Source finding context: SKU dimensions are stored as a string with source-system-dependent units. Source finding context: Shipment total weight is computed and stored without unit, package hierarchy, or dimensional-weight model.
  - affected purpose: Use the ontology as the concept baseline for unit-consistent WMS/TMS/ERP integration and logistics risk control. / Freight, warehouse, and TMS integration concept baseline.
  - failure condition: When SKUs from different warehouses or source systems are combined into shipments, total_weight may sum values recorded in incompatible or unknown units. / When SKUs or shipments cross warehouses/carriers using different measurement conventions, the ontology cannot determine the unit, measurement basis, or package level of weight/dimensions.
  - impact: The model can produce incorrect shipment weight and dimensions, weakening carrier integration, cost calculation, load planning, and operational trust in the reference ontology. / Carrier rating, routing, capacity planning, and reconciliation can be wrong because numeric measurements are not semantically comparable across systems.
  - root hypothesis: Freight measurement failures stem from representing logistics measurements as bare numbers or strings instead of structured value/unit/source measurements.

Value authority anchor: .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/review-value-alignment-criteria.yaml:6, statement requires a WMS/TMS/ERP integration concept standard centered on unit consistency and logistics operating risk.`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-005`, `Sku `weight` note: warehouse-specific kg or lb convention is used`, `Sku `dims` note: dimensions are a string and units vary by source system`, `Shipment `total_weight` note: computed sum stored at shipment confirmation`, `Embedded materialized input: Sku `weight` note; Sku `dims` note; Shipment `total_weight` note`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-002`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-009`, `rel-010`, `finding-relation-graph.yaml#rel-010`, `finding-002.cause-001`, `finding-002.cause-002`, `finding-002.cause-003`, `finding-009.cause-001`, `Embedded materialized input: `Sku.weight` note`, `finding-009.cause-002`, `Embedded materialized input: `Sku.dims` note`, `finding-009.cause-003`, `Embedded materialized input: `Shipment.total_weight` note`, `Embedded materialized input: entities list lacks package/handling-unit entity`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-002.axiology`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-002`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-002.coverage`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-002.evolution`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/issue-ledger.yaml#issue-002`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-002`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-002.logic`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/finding-relation-graph.yaml#rel-010`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-002.semantics`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-003`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-002.structure`, `issue-ledger.yaml#dep-002`, `rel-012`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: axiology, coverage
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - materiality: This is material because the declared purpose depends on unit consistency and logistics operating risk control. If SKU measurements from different warehouses or source systems can be combined without known units or basis, shipment totals can be numerically valid-looking but operationally wrong, weakening carrier rating, routing, capacity planning, cost calculation, reconciliation, and trust in the ontology.
  - root cause: The starting point is the ontology's choice to represent measurements as source-dependent bare values instead of a canonical Measurement concept. Once unit, source, precision, conversion policy, and package level are absent at the SKU and dimension fields, downstream shipment calculations have no authoritative semantics to aggregate or compare.
  - causal path: The causal chain begins with Sku.weight and Sku.dims allowing warehouse-specific or source-system-dependent units. Shipment.total_weight then sums line weights and stores the result without unit, derivation source, timestamp, package hierarchy, or dimensional-weight basis. That propagates local measurement ambiguity into shipment-level values used by freight and warehouse integrations.
  - action: Fix this before the next stage by introducing normalized measurement concepts for weight and dimensions, including value, unit of measure, precision, source, effective time, and conversion policy. Define canonical integration units for shipment totals, store total_weight with unit and derivation metadata, and attach gross, net, dimensional weight, and dimensions to the correct package or handling-unit level such as carton, pallet, parcel, or shipment container.

Source finding context: TrackingEvent.event_type and independent status management rule Source finding context: Target: .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:44, .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:63, .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:66, .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:112. Source finding context: The declared purpose asks whether this is a concept standard for integration, especially status model and authority. By leaving status correspondence to per-integration decisions, the ontology avoids the exact cross-system alignment it is supposed to provide. This permits inconsistent delivered/exception/in_transit interpretations across consumers. Source finding context: Consumers can disagree about shipment state and terminal delivery, weakening operational coordination, customer notifications, exception management, and auditability. Source finding context: The ontology treats cross-system status alignment as an adapter responsibility rather than a central concept-standard responsibility. Source finding context: Shipment.status and TrackingEvent.event_type are separate status vocabularies. Source finding context: Carrier-status mapping is explicitly delegated to each integration, and OMS/TMS/carrier status fields are independently managed. Source finding context: Because status model and authority are declared review-purpose criteria, the delegation weakens the ontology's suitability as a shared integration standard.   - impact: Consumers can disagree about shipment state and terminal delivery, weakening operational coordination, customer notifications, exception management, and auditability. / 상태 의미가 연동별로 갈라져 동일 배송의 진행·예외 판단을 공통 기준으로 유지하기 어렵고, 외부 변경 때 기존 데이터와 새 데이터의 상태 해석 연속성이 깨질 수 있습니다.
  - root hypothesis: Status-model inconsistency and evolution fragility both stem from lacking a versioned canonical status/event mapping between external carrier codes and internal shipment states.

  - source lenses: axiology, evolution
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - materiality: This weakens the declared purpose of serving as the WMS/TMS/ERP integration status-model and authority baseline. If carrier event meanings and shipment status transitions are left to each integration, consumers can disagree on in_transit, exception, delivered, and other terminal or non-happy-path states, undermining coordination, customer notifications, exception handling, and auditability.
  - root cause: The starting point is the absence of a versioned canonical StatusMapping or EventNormalization concept between external carrier codes and internal shipment states. Without that authority layer, status meaning is owned by adapters and fixed enums rather than by the ontology's shared integration model.
  - causal path: Shipment.status and TrackingEvent.event_type are modeled as separate fixed vocabularies. Carrier-status mapping is then delegated to individual integrations, while OMS, TMS, and carrier status fields are managed independently. That structure allows inconsistent internal state derivation today and makes future carrier codes, exceptions, returns, re-delivery, lost shipment, or correction states require enum changes or integration-by-integration reinterpretation.
  - action: Define a canonical lifecycle/status mapping table before treating the ontology as the shared status baseline. The table should separate external carrier_event_code from canonical shipment state, include mapping versions, transition rules, terminal-state precedence, exception handling, and the authority allowed to advance or correct each state; stable enums should remain high-level categories while detailed external codes live in extensible mapping/code-set structures.

- issue-004 (medium): Issue-004 is a material traceability gap: the ontology needs a line-level fulfillment/allocation authority, not only a Shipment-to-Order fulfillment relation, to serve as an order, freight, and delivery integration standard.
  - issue stance agreement: 6/6
  - agreed or narrowed lenses: axiology, evolution, logic, structure, coverage, semantics

  - raised by lenses: axiology
  - issue statement: Omitting OrderLine-to-shipment fulfillment mapping is misaligned with the ontology's fulfillment integration purpose because it sacrifices operational traceability for a simpler order-level relation. Source finding context: OrderLine fulfillment traceability through Shipment / DeliveryLeg Source finding context: Target: .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:25, .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:30, .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:105, .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:109. Value authority anchor: .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/review-value-alignment-criteria.yaml:6, statement requires an order/inventory/shipment/delivery concept standard focused on logistics operating risk. Source finding context: A logistics fulfillment concept baseline must support the operational question of which ordered item moved in which shipment, especially for partial shipments and exceptions. The model chooses a simpler order-level relation, but that local simplification weakens the broader purpose of coordinating orders, freight, and delivery across systems. Source finding context: Add a fulfillment allocation/detail concept linking OrderLine to Shipment or DeliveryLeg with shipped quantity, split/partial flags, timestamps, and exception/cancellation state. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/axiology.findings.yaml#axiology-candidate-004 Source finding context: Use the ontology as a concept standard across order, freight, and delivery workflows while controlling logistics operating risk. Source finding context: An order is partially shipped, split across shipments, or has an exception on one line, but the ontology only records a Shipment-to-Order relation. Source finding context: The reference model cannot explain which ordered quantities were fulfilled by which physical movements, undermining exception resolution, customer service, reconciliation, and auditability. Source finding context: The ontology optimizes for order-level fulfillment simplicity instead of preserving line-level operational traceability. Source finding context: OrderLine contains product and quantity but no fulfillment shipment or leg reference. Source finding context: The only fulfills relation is Shipment to Order and explicitly omits OrderLine-level mapping. Source finding context: Because the declared review purpose is suitability as an order, freight, and delivery integration standard with logistics risk focus, this omission weakens purpose alignment.
  - affected purpose: Use the ontology as a concept standard across order, freight, and delivery workflows while controlling logistics operating risk.
  - failure condition: An order is partially shipped, split across shipments, or has an exception on one line, but the ontology only records a Shipment-to-Order relation.
  - impact: The reference model cannot explain which ordered quantities were fulfilled by which physical movements, undermining exception resolution, customer service, reconciliation, and auditability.
  - root hypothesis: The ontology optimizes for order-level fulfillment simplicity instead of preserving line-level operational traceability.

Value authority anchor: .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/review-value-alignment-criteria.yaml:6, statement requires an order/inventory/shipment/delivery concept standard focused on logistics operating risk.`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-004`, `finding-004.cause-001`, `finding-004.cause-002`, `finding-004.cause-003`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-004.axiology`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-004`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-004.coverage`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-001`, `relations `fulfills`: Shipment to Order only; note says no OrderLine-level mapping`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-004.evolution`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/issue-ledger.yaml#issue-004`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-001`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-004.logic`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-004.semantics`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-004.structure`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: axiology
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, accept_risk
  - materiality: The declared purpose is to support order, freight, and delivery workflows while controlling logistics operating risk. When fulfillment is recorded only at order level, the ontology cannot answer which ordered item quantity moved in which shipment or delivery leg, especially in partial shipments, split fulfillment, exceptions, cancellations, reconciliation, customer service, or audit scenarios. That weakens the ontology's operational integration value rather than merely leaving out a convenience detail.
  - root cause: The accepted root is the absence of a line-level fulfillment/allocation concept that links OrderLine quantities to Shipment or DeliveryLeg evidence. This is the starting point because OrderLine carries product and quantity, but fulfillment evidence is attached only to the order as a whole, so all downstream traceability questions lose the line and quantity authority they require.
  - causal path: The causal path is: OrderLine has product and quantity without a shipment or leg reference; the only fulfillment relation links Shipment to Order and explicitly omits OrderLine-level mapping; therefore partial, split, exception, reconciliation, and audit cases collapse into an order-level fulfilled/not-fulfilled view. The lenses converged that this missing allocation/detail structure causes the observed operational traceability failure, while narrowing the framing from a broad simplicity tradeoff to the specific absent line-level authority.
  - action: Add a fulfillment allocation/detail concept linking OrderLine to Shipment or DeliveryLeg, including shipped quantity, split or partial flags, timestamps, and exception or cancellation state. This should be closed before the next stage because later fulfillment, exception, reconciliation, and audit semantics depend on this authority being present rather than inferred from order-level shipment links.
  - unresolved disagreement: All participating lenses accepted the missing line-level allocation concept as the actionable root, but severity remains unresolved: axiology, semantics, and logic keep the medium framing, while coverage, evolution, and structure support raising severity because of reconciliation, exception handling, auditability, and future fulfillment patterns.

- issue-006 (medium): The ontology’s order and shipment lifecycles are materially incomplete because they stop at normal forward progress to delivered while tracking events can already report exceptions. This leaves cancellation, failed delivery, return, reissue, correction, reversal, and archival closure without canonical lifecycle representation.
  - issue stance agreement: 6/6
  - agreed or narrowed lenses: axiology, coverage, logic, semantics, evolution, structure
  - issue stance disagreement: 0/6
  - disagreeing stance lenses: none
  - not applicable lenses: none
  - insufficient evidence lenses: none
  - resolution accepted by: 1/1 deliberation participants
  - accepted lenses: coverage
  - remaining disagreement: 0/1 deliberation participants
  - remaining disagreement lenses: none
  - raised by lenses: coverage
  - issue statement: Order and Shipment lifecycle coverage omits cancellation, failed delivery, return, reissue, correction, reversal, and archival phases despite TrackingEvent supporting exceptions. Source finding context: Embedded materialized input: Order `fulfillment_status` values; Shipment `status` values and note; TrackingEvent `event_type` values Source finding context: Lifecycle states stop at happy-path delivery and omit cancellation, exception resolution, return, correction, and archival phases. Source finding context: A logistics fulfillment baseline must cover operational endings and post-final corrections. The current state models treat `delivered` as final while still having carrier exceptions and multi-system updates elsewhere, so real operations such as failed delivery, return-to-sender, cancellation after allocation, shipment reissue, proof-of-delivery correction, and audit closure are outside the conceptual lifecycle. Source finding context: Extend lifecycle coverage with explicit terminal and post-terminal concepts: canceled/backordered for orders or lines; exception/failed_delivery/returned/reissued for shipments; correction or reversal events; and archival/audit-closed states with rules for how post-delivery events affect status. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-004 Source finding context: Order, freight, and delivery concept consistency for logistics operations. Source finding context: When fulfillment leaves the happy path or a delivered shipment must be corrected, the ontology has no lifecycle concept to represent the condition without overloading existing states. Source finding context: Operations risk is hidden because exceptions and reversals cannot be represented consistently across OMS, TMS, carrier tracking, and ERP reconciliation. Source finding context: Lifecycle enumerations are limited to forward-progress delivery states and define delivered as final. Source finding context: Order and shipment statuses enumerate only normal forward progress to delivered. Source finding context: Carrier tracking can report `exception`, but shipment status has no exception or recovery state. Source finding context: The ontology therefore does not cover cancellation, failed delivery, returns, reissue, correction, or archival lifecycle phases.
  - affected purpose: Order, freight, and delivery concept consistency for logistics operations and WMS/TMS/ERP reconciliation. Source finding context: Order, freight, and delivery concept consistency for logistics operations.
  - failure condition: Fulfillment leaves the happy path, a delivered shipment must be corrected, or cancellation/return/reissue needs to be represented without overloading existing statuses. Source finding context: When fulfillment leaves the happy path or a delivered shipment must be corrected, the ontology has no lifecycle concept to represent the condition without overloading existing states.
  - impact: Exceptions and reversals cannot be represented consistently across OMS, TMS, carrier tracking, and ERP reconciliation, hiding operational risk. Source finding context: Operations risk is hidden because exceptions and reversals cannot be represented consistently across OMS, TMS, carrier tracking, and ERP reconciliation.
  - root hypothesis: Lifecycle enumerations are limited to forward-progress delivery states and define delivered as final, leaving non-happy-path and post-terminal logistics states outside the model.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-004`, `Order `fulfillment_status` values: received, allocated, picking, shipped, delivered`, `Shipment `status` values: created, picked, in_transit, delivered; note says delivered is final`, `TrackingEvent `event_type` includes exception`, `Embedded materialized input: Order `fulfillment_status` values; Shipment `status` values and note; TrackingEvent `event_type` values`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-008`, `finding-008.cause-001`, `Embedded materialized input: `Order.fulfillment_status` values`, `Embedded materialized input: `Shipment.status` values`, `finding-008.cause-002`, `Embedded materialized input: `TrackingEvent.event_type` values`, `finding-008.cause-003`, `Embedded materialized input: entity/status definitions contain no cancellation/return/correction/archive states`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-006.axiology`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-006`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-006.coverage`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-006.evolution`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/issue-ledger.yaml#issue-006`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-003`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-006.logic`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-006.semantics`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-004`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-006.structure`, `issue-ledger.yaml#dep-003`, `rel-016`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: coverage
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - materiality: This weakens order, freight, and delivery concept consistency for logistics operations and WMS/TMS/ERP reconciliation because non-happy-path outcomes and post-delivery corrections cannot be represented consistently. When OMS, TMS, carrier tracking, and ERP systems need to coordinate an exception, reversal, or correction, the model forces those conditions into happy-path statuses or leaves them outside the lifecycle, hiding operational risk.
  - root cause: The starting point is the fixed lifecycle enumeration: order and shipment statuses cover only forward movement toward delivery, and shipment delivery is treated as final. Because the lifecycle itself has no terminal, recovery, or post-terminal concepts, downstream handling for exception, return, correction, reissue, or audit closure has no stable status meaning to attach to.
  - causal path: The causal path is straightforward: Order fulfillment statuses run from received through delivered, and Shipment statuses run from created through delivered. TrackingEvent can report an exception, but Shipment has no corresponding exception or recovery state. As a result, the ontology can observe that something went wrong while lacking lifecycle states for cancellation, failed delivery, return, reissue, correction, reversal, or archival closure.
  - action: Extend lifecycle coverage before relying on the ontology as an operational standard. Add explicit non-happy-path, terminal, and post-terminal concepts such as canceled or backordered order states, failed_delivery, returned, reissued, exception or recovery shipment states, correction or reversal events, and archival or audit-closed states, with rules for how exception and correction events affect order, shipment, and audit status.

  - action candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - materiality: 선언된 목적은 재고 할당과 WMS/ERP 재고 권위 통합 기준입니다. 현재 모델은 주문 일중에도 야간 InventoryAggregate.available_qty를 읽고, InventoryRecord.quantity_on_hand도 현재 보유 수량 중심이라 주문 당시 재고 판단, 이후 조정, ERP 기준 reconcile 결과를 같은 수량 표면에 섞게 됩니다. 그래서 실시간 원천이나 감사 가능한 재고 이력을 추가하면 기존 주문 판단을 재현하기 어렵고, 새 재고 원천과의 통합 연속성도 약해집니다.
  - root cause: 시작점은 Inventory quantity concepts lack structured effective time and source authority입니다. 수량 자체에 as_of/effective_at 같은 유효 시점과 source_system 같은 권위 원천이 붙어 있지 않으면, WMS 물리 재고, ERP 회계 재고, 야간 스냅샷, 실시간 이벤트가 서로 다른 주장인지 같은 값의 갱신인지 구분할 기준이 없습니다.
  - causal path: InventoryRecord.quantity_on_hand는 WMS와 ERP가 각자 값을 가진 뒤 야간 배치로 ERP 기준에 맞춰지는 현재 수량이고, InventoryAggregate.available_qty는 매일 밤 저장되는 SKU별 전사 가용 재고 스냅샷입니다. 그런데 재고 할당은 일중 주문에서도 이 nightly snapshot 값을 읽습니다. 동시에 as_of 개념 부재가 명시되어 주문 접수 당시 재고 같은 시점 질의가 불가능하므로, 실시간 또는 감사 가능한 재고 체계로 확장할 때 기존 현재값과 야간값 필드의 의미를 재정의해야 하는 causal path가 성립합니다.
  - action: InventoryRecord와 InventoryAggregate에 as_of/effective_at, source_system, snapshot_type, reconciliation_status를 도입하고, 할당은 단순 현재 스냅샷 값이 아니라 특정 시점의 가용성 이벤트 또는 재고 원장 기준을 참조하게 해야 합니다. 이 변경은 다음 단계 통합 전에 닫아야 합니다. 먼저 수량의 시간·원천 권위를 모델의 기준 개념으로 세워야 이후 실시간 WMS, ERP 조정, 주문 시점 감사, 회계 마감 재고가 기존 데이터 의미를 덮어쓰지 않고 연결됩니다.

- issue-009 (medium): issue-009 is a material medium issue: DeliveryLeg is modeled as a subtype of Shipment even though it represents route segments, so route reasoning needs composition and ordered containment under a parent Shipment or Route instead of inheritance.
  - issue stance agreement: 5/6
  - agreed or narrowed lenses: axiology, evolution, logic, semantics, structure
  - issue stance disagreement: 0/6
  - disagreeing stance lenses: none
  - not applicable lenses: none
  - insufficient evidence lenses: coverage
  - resolution accepted by: 6/6 deliberation participants
  - accepted lenses: logic, structure, coverage, axiology, evolution, semantics
  - remaining disagreement: 0/6 deliberation participants
  - remaining disagreement lenses: none
  - raised by lenses: logic, structure
  - issue statement: Shared-root issue across finding-014, finding-020: DeliveryLeg route reasoning is unstable because the ontology uses inheritance where it needs shipment-route composition and ordering. Source finding context: logistics-fulfillment-ontology.yaml / entities.Shipment and entities.DeliveryLeg Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:40, .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:52 Source finding context: DeliveryLeg is declared as a Shipment while its definition allows route segments that are not complete warehouse-to-customer shipments. Source finding context: This is an inter-claim necessary-type conflict: the subtype declaration requires all DeliveryLeg instances to be Shipment instances, while the DeliveryLeg definition permits instances whose origin/destination pattern does not meet the Shipment definition. It matters for WMS/TMS/ERP concept integration because code or mappings that inherit Shipment obligations onto each DeliveryLeg can treat a partial leg as a full customer shipment, weakening status, carrier, and fulfillment reasoning. Source finding context: Make `Shipment` the whole consignment/order movement and model `DeliveryLeg` as a component of Shipment instead of `is_a`, or broaden `Shipment` to mean any physical cargo movement and introduce a separate whole-shipment concept for warehouse-to-customer fulfillment. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/logic.findings.yaml#logic-candidate-001 Source finding context: Concept baseline for WMS/TMS/ERP integration across order, freight, and delivery concepts. Source finding context: When DeliveryLeg instances such as hub-to-customer are interpreted through the `is_a: Shipment` declaration, they must also satisfy the narrower Shipment definition of warehouse-to-customer movement. Source finding context: The ontology can classify partial route legs as full shipments, making downstream integration rules about shipment status, fulfillment, and delivery scope logically unstable. Source finding context: The ontology uses inheritance for a part-of route segment relationship while keeping the parent definition narrower than the child’s allowed instances. Source finding context: `Shipment` is defined as movement from warehouse to customer. Source finding context: `DeliveryLeg` is declared `is_a: Shipment`. Source finding context: `DeliveryLeg` permits route segments such as warehouse-to-hub and hub-to-customer, which are not necessarily full warehouse-to-customer movements. Source finding context: logistics-fulfillment-ontology.yaml / DeliveryLeg 경로 구성 구조 Source finding context: materialized-input.md: Shipment definition says DeliveryLeg is also a kind of Shipment; DeliveryLeg has leg_seq, from_ref, and to_ref; relations contains only fulfills from Shipment to Order and no parent shipment, route, or next-leg relation. Source finding context: DeliveryLeg has no structural link that groups legs into a shipment route. Source finding context: A sequence number is only meaningful within a containing route or shipment. Because DeliveryLeg is modeled only as an is_a Shipment subtype, each leg inherits shipment-level behavior but lacks a structural edge that says which shipment route it belongs to or which legs precede/follow it. Source finding context: Introduce a parent/composition relation such as Shipment has_many DeliveryLeg, DeliveryLeg belongs_to Shipment, or a separate Route entity with ordered legs; scope leg_seq to that parent. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/structure.findings.yaml#structure-candidate-002 Source finding context: Concept reference document for shipment and delivery integration between WMS/TMS/carrier systems. Source finding context: When a delivery has multiple legs, consumers cannot reconstruct the end-to-end route or associate leg-level tracking and locations with one parent shipment. Source finding context: TMS/carrier integration loses a stable structural path for multi-leg delivery state, ETA, and exception handling, even though DeliveryLeg is declared as part of the shipment model. Source finding context: The ontology uses inheritance to relate DeliveryLeg to Shipment but omits the composition or ordering relation required for route-level structure. Source finding context: DeliveryLeg is a route segment with leg_seq/from/to attributes. Source finding context: No explicit relation groups DeliveryLeg instances under a parent shipment or route. Source finding context: The is_a Shipment link makes each leg a kind of shipment but does not provide route containment or adjacency.
  - affected purpose: Concept baseline for WMS/TMS/ERP integration across order, freight, and delivery concepts. / Concept reference document for shipment and delivery integration between WMS/TMS/carrier systems.
  - failure condition: When DeliveryLeg instances such as hub-to-customer are interpreted through the `is_a: Shipment` declaration, they must also satisfy the narrower Shipment definition of warehouse-to-customer movement. / When a delivery has multiple legs, consumers cannot reconstruct the end-to-end route or associate leg-level tracking and locations with one parent shipment.
  - impact: The ontology can classify partial route legs as full shipments, making downstream integration rules about shipment status, fulfillment, and delivery scope logically unstable. / TMS/carrier integration loses a stable structural path for multi-leg delivery state, ETA, and exception handling, even though DeliveryLeg is declared as part of the shipment model.
  - root hypothesis: DeliveryLeg route reasoning is unstable because the ontology uses inheritance where it needs shipment-route composition and ordering.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/logic.findings.yaml#logic-candidate-001`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:40-49`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:52-58`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:40, .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:52`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/structure.findings.yaml#structure-candidate-002`, `materialized-input.md: DeliveryLeg definition says it is one segment of a cargo route.`, `materialized-input.md: DeliveryLeg attributes include leg_seq, from_ref, and to_ref but no parent shipment or route reference.`, `materialized-input.md: relations contains only fulfills from Shipment to Order.`, `materialized-input.md: Shipment definition says DeliveryLeg is also a kind of Shipment; DeliveryLeg has leg_seq, from_ref, and to_ref; relations contains only fulfills from Shipment to Order and no parent shipment, route, or next-leg relation.`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-014`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-020`, `rel-018`, `finding-relation-graph.yaml#rel-018`, `finding-014.cause-001`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:40-41`, `finding-014.cause-002`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:52-54`, `finding-014.cause-003`, `finding-020.cause-001`, `materialized-input.md: DeliveryLeg definition and attributes.`, `finding-020.cause-002`, `materialized-input.md: DeliveryLeg attributes do not include parent shipment or route reference.`, `finding-020.cause-003`, `materialized-input.md: Shipment definition says DeliveryLeg is also a kind of Shipment.`, `materialized-input.md: DeliveryLeg has is_a: Shipment.`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-009.axiology`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-009`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-009.coverage`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-009.evolution`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/issue-ledger.yaml#issue-009`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-009.logic`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/finding-relation-graph.yaml#rel-018`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-009.semantics`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-002`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-009.structure`, `issue-ledger.yaml#dep-004`, `rel-019`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: logic, structure
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - materiality: This weakens the declared WMS/TMS/ERP integration purpose because downstream systems can treat partial legs, such as warehouse-to-hub or hub-to-customer movements, as complete customer shipments. That destabilizes shipment status, fulfillment scope, carrier handoff, ETA, and exception reasoning, and also prevents consumers from reconstructing a multi-leg route under one stable parent shipment.
  - root cause: The starting point is the part-whole modeling error: Shipment is the whole consignment or order movement, while DeliveryLeg is a segment of that movement. Declaring DeliveryLeg as `is_a: Shipment` transfers whole-shipment obligations to each leg, but it does not create the containment or route scope that leg sequencing requires.
  - causal path: The evidence chain is that Shipment is defined as warehouse-to-customer movement, DeliveryLeg is declared `is_a: Shipment`, and DeliveryLeg permits segment endpoints such as warehouse-to-hub and hub-to-customer that are not necessarily full shipments. Structurally, DeliveryLeg has `leg_seq`, `from_ref`, and `to_ref`, but no parent shipment, route, or adjacency relation; therefore sequence has no containing scope and each leg inherits shipment-level meaning without being placed in an end-to-end route.
  - action: Fix the model before the next stage by choosing a clear route authority: either keep Shipment as the whole consignment/order movement and model DeliveryLeg as a contained component of Shipment, or broaden Shipment to any cargo movement and introduce a separate whole-shipment fulfillment concept. In either case, add an explicit parent/composition relation, such as Shipment has_many DeliveryLeg, DeliveryLeg belongs_to Shipment, or a Route entity with ordered legs, and scope `leg_seq` to that parent.

- issue-011 (medium): DeliveryLeg를 Shipment의 하위 유형으로 둔 현재 모델은 배송 구간과 전체 출하를 같은 종류로 취급하므로 수정이 필요하다. DeliveryLeg는 Shipment 자체가 아니라 Shipment 또는 Route에 속한 route segment로 분리되어야 한다.

  - not applicable lenses: none
  - insufficient evidence lenses: coverage

  - issue statement: DeliveryLeg를 Shipment의 하위 유형으로 둔 것은 구간과 전체 화물 이동을 혼동한다. Source finding context: entities.DeliveryLeg.is_a Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md: Shipment definition "창고에서 고객으로 가는 물리적 화물 이동. 배송 구간(DeliveryLeg)도 Shipment의 일종이다."; DeliveryLeg definition "화물 경로의 한 구간(예: 창고→허브, 허브→고객)."; relation fulfills from Shipment to Order Source finding context: 구간은 전체 출하와 부분-전체 관계에 가깝다. 하위 유형으로 두면 창고→허브 구간도 고객 주문을 충족하는 Shipment처럼 해석될 수 있고, 각 leg의 delivered 상태와 전체 shipment/order delivered 상태가 의미적으로 뒤섞인다. Source finding context: DeliveryLeg를 Shipment의 subclass가 아니라 Shipment에 속한 route segment로 모델링한다. 예: DeliveryLeg.parent_shipment_ref 또는 Shipment.legs를 두고, fulfills 관계는 전체 Shipment에만 유지한다. Leg 상태는 구간 완료 상태로 별도 정의한다. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-002 Source finding context: 화물·배송 개념의 정합성을 제공하여 TMS/OMS/WMS 상태와 관계를 통합하는 목적. Source finding context: 하나의 주문 출하가 여러 구간을 거치거나 중간 허브 구간 상태를 추적할 때 DeliveryLeg가 Shipment로 취급된다. Source finding context: 구간 완료와 전체 배송 완료, 구간 운송과 주문 충족이 같은 의미 계층으로 섞여 상태 매핑과 주문 충족 판단이 부정확해질 수 있다. Source finding context: 배송 구간이라는 부분 개념을 전체 출하 이동의 특수한 종류로 분류했다. Source finding context: DeliveryLeg는 화물 경로의 한 구간으로 정의된다. Source finding context: DeliveryLeg는 is_a: Shipment로 선언되어 전체 Shipment의 의미와 관계를 상속한다. Source finding context: Shipment는 Order를 fulfills하는 관계의 출발점이므로 DeliveryLeg도 주문 충족 주체처럼 해석될 수 있다.
  - affected purpose: 화물·배송 개념의 정합성을 제공하여 TMS/OMS/WMS 상태와 관계를 통합하는 목적.
  - failure condition: 하나의 주문 출하가 여러 구간을 거치거나 중간 허브 구간 상태를 추적할 때 DeliveryLeg가 Shipment로 취급된다.
  - impact: 구간 완료와 전체 배송 완료, 구간 운송과 주문 충족이 같은 의미 계층으로 섞여 상태 매핑과 주문 충족 판단이 부정확해질 수 있다.
  - root hypothesis: 배송 구간이라는 부분 개념을 전체 출하 이동의 특수한 종류로 분류했다.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-002`, `materialized-input.md Shipment definition`, `materialized-input.md DeliveryLeg definition`, `materialized-input.md relations fulfills`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md: Shipment definition "창고에서 고객으로 가는 물리적 화물 이동. 배송 구간(DeliveryLeg)도 Shipment의 일종이다."; DeliveryLeg definition "화물 경로의 한 구간(예: 창고→허브, 허브→고객)."; relation fulfills from Shipment to Order`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-016`, `finding-016.cause-001`, `finding-016.cause-002`, `materialized-input.md DeliveryLeg.is_a`, `finding-016.cause-003`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-011.axiology`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-011`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-011.coverage`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-011.evolution`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/issue-ledger.yaml#issue-011`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-011.logic`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/finding-relation-graph.yaml#rel-017`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-011.semantics`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-011.structure`, `issue-ledger.yaml#dep-004`, `rel-019`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: semantics
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - materiality: 이 문제는 TMS/OMS/WMS 간 화물·배송 상태와 관계를 정합적으로 통합하려는 목적을 약화시킨다. 하나의 주문 출하가 여러 구간을 거칠 때 DeliveryLeg가 Shipment 의미를 상속하면 구간 완료가 전체 배송 완료처럼 보이고, 구간 운송이 주문 충족 주체처럼 해석되어 상태 매핑과 fulfillment 판단이 부정확해질 수 있다.
  - root cause: 시작점은 배송 구간이라는 부분 개념을 전체 출하 이동의 특수한 종류로 분류한 것이다. DeliveryLeg는 경로의 한 구간으로 정의되지만 is_a: Shipment로 선언되어 Shipment의 전체 이동 의미와 관계를 상속하게 되므로, 이후의 상태·관계 혼동이 이 타입 모델링 오류에서 파생된다.
  - causal path: 근거 흐름은 DeliveryLeg가 화물 경로의 한 구간으로 정의된 데서 시작한다. 그런데 이를 Shipment의 하위 유형으로 두면서 전체 Shipment의 의미를 상속하고, Shipment가 Order를 fulfills하는 관계의 출발점이기 때문에 DeliveryLeg도 주문을 충족하는 주체처럼 해석될 수 있다. 이 경로가 구간 완료, 전체 배송 완료, 주문 충족 상태가 같은 의미 계층에서 뒤섞이는 문제를 만든다.
  - action: DeliveryLeg를 Shipment의 subclass에서 제거하고 Shipment.legs 또는 DeliveryLeg.parent_shipment_ref 같은 포함·연결 관계로 모델링해야 한다. fulfills 관계는 전체 Shipment에만 유지하고, DeliveryLeg의 상태는 leg-specific 완료 또는 운송 상태로 별도 정의해야 한다. 이 변경은 다음 단계 전에 닫아야 하는 구조적 전제이며, 구간 상태와 전체 출하/주문 충족 상태의 해석 경계를 먼저 고정해야 이후 통합 매핑이 안정된다.

- issue-012 (medium): The ontology must treat measurement units as part of the measurement value model. As written, SKU weight, SKU dimensions, and shipment total weight cannot remain stable when warehouses, carriers, regions, or rating engines use different unit conventions.
  - issue stance agreement: 6/6
  - agreed or narrowed lenses: axiology, evolution, logic, semantics, structure, coverage
  - issue stance disagreement: 0/6
  - disagreeing stance lenses: none
  - not applicable lenses: none
  - insufficient evidence lenses: none
  - resolution accepted by: 2/2 deliberation participants
  - accepted lenses: semantics, evolution
  - remaining disagreement: 0/2 deliberation participants
  - remaining disagreement lenses: none
  - raised by lenses: semantics, evolution
  - issue statement: Shared-root issue across finding-017, finding-011: The unit model is not evolvable because measurement unit is not part of the measurement value concept. Source finding context: entities.Sku.attributes.weight, entities.Sku.attributes.dims, entities.Shipment.attributes.total_weight Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md: Sku.weight note "창고별 계량 관례(kg 또는 lb)를 그대로 쓴다."; Sku.dims note "단위는 소스 시스템마다 다르다."; Shipment.total_weight note "구성 OrderLine 중량의 합." Source finding context: weight/dims가 단위 없는 숫자·문자열로 정의되어 Shipment.total_weight의 의미가 안정적인 중량 합계가 될 수 없다. Source finding context: 중량과 치수는 단위가 의미의 일부다. kg와 lb가 같은 number 필드에 섞이면 total_weight라는 파생값은 같은 이름이어도 합산 기준이 불명확하다. WMS/TMS/ERP 통합에서 운임 산정, 적재 제한, 라벨/운송사 전송에 쓰이는 물류 수치가 의미적으로 비교 불가능해진다. Source finding context: 측정값을 value + unit 구조로 표준화하고 canonical unit 변환 규칙을 둔다. Shipment.total_weight는 normalized_weight 합계인지 원천 측정값인지 명시하고, 가능하면 source weights와 calculated_at/source_basis를 함께 둔다. dims도 length/width/height 각각 value+unit으로 분해한다. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-003 Source finding context: 주문·재고·화물·배송 개념의 단위 정합성을 보장하는 통합 기준 문서 목적. Source finding context: SKU 중량이나 치수 원천이 kg/lb 또는 서로 다른 길이 단위를 섞어 제공되는 경우 Shipment.total_weight가 구성 OrderLine 중량의 합으로 계산된다. Source finding context: 동일한 total_weight 값이 어떤 단위의 합인지 알 수 없어 운송사, 창고, ERP 간 물류 수치 매핑과 검증이 신뢰하기 어렵다. Source finding context: 측정 단위를 독립된 의미 요소로 모델링하지 않고 소스 시스템 관례에 맡긴다. Source finding context: Sku.weight는 number로만 선언되고 kg 또는 lb를 창고별 관례대로 사용한다고 설명된다. Source finding context: Sku.dims도 단위가 소스 시스템마다 다른 문자열로 선언된다. Source finding context: Shipment.total_weight는 구성 OrderLine 중량의 합으로 저장되므로 단위가 다른 원천값을 같은 합계 개념으로 만들 수 있다. Source finding context: logistics-fulfillment-ontology.yaml Sku weight/dims and Shipment.total_weight Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:33-38,40-49 Source finding context: 단위가 값과 분리되어 있지 않아 새 창고·운송사·국가 단위계를 추가하면 기존 수치 데이터의 연속성이 깨진다. Source finding context: 확장 관점에서 새 WMS, TMS, 운송사 요율, 국가권역을 붙일 때 중량과 치수는 라벨 발행, 요율 계산, 적재 제한, 통관 확장의 핵심 입력입니다. 현재 숫자와 문자열은 단위 권위를 포함하지 않아 새 단위계를 수용하려면 과거 값의 의미를 재해석하거나 기존 필드를 마이그레이션해야 합니다. Source finding context: 중량과 치수를 value+unit 구조로 모델링하고, 원천 시스템 단위와 표준화 단위를 구분합니다. Shipment.total_weight도 계산 기준 단위와 계산 시점, 원천 SKU 버전을 함께 보존하도록 바꿉니다. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-002 Source finding context: WMS/TMS/ERP 통합에서 SKU, 화물, 배송 계산의 공통 개념 기준 Source finding context: kg/lb 또는 cm/in가 섞인 창고, 운송사, 국가권역, 요율 엔진을 추가하는 경우 Source finding context: 동일 필드의 숫자가 시스템마다 다른 의미를 가져 기존 데이터와 새 데이터의 비교, 합산, 요율 계산 연속성이 약해집니다. Source finding context: 측정값 개념이 단위와 원천 단위 권위를 속성으로 갖지 않는다. Source finding context: Sku.weight는 숫자만 있고 창고별 kg 또는 lb 관례를 그대로 사용한다고 명시한다. Source finding context: Sku.dims는 단위가 소스 시스템마다 다른 문자열로 저장된다. Source finding context: Shipment.total_weight는 OrderLine 중량의 합으로 저장되지만 단위 또는 변환 기준을 표현하지 않는다. Source finding context: 따라서 새 단위계가 들어오면 기존 필드 의미를 유지한 채 확장하기 어렵고 과거 데이터 해석도 흔들린다.

  - failure condition: SKU 중량이나 치수 원천이 kg/lb 또는 서로 다른 길이 단위를 섞어 제공되는 경우 Shipment.total_weight가 구성 OrderLine 중량의 합으로 계산된다. / kg/lb 또는 cm/in가 섞인 창고, 운송사, 국가권역, 요율 엔진을 추가하는 경우
  - impact: 동일한 total_weight 값이 어떤 단위의 합인지 알 수 없어 운송사, 창고, ERP 간 물류 수치 매핑과 검증이 신뢰하기 어렵다. / 동일 필드의 숫자가 시스템마다 다른 의미를 가져 기존 데이터와 새 데이터의 비교, 합산, 요율 계산 연속성이 약해집니다.
  - root hypothesis: The unit model is not evolvable because measurement unit is not part of the measurement value concept.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-003`, `materialized-input.md Sku.weight note`, `materialized-input.md Sku.dims note`, `materialized-input.md Shipment.total_weight note`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md: Sku.weight note "창고별 계량 관례(kg 또는 lb)를 그대로 쓴다."; Sku.dims note "단위는 소스 시스템마다 다르다."; Shipment.total_weight note "구성 OrderLine 중량의 합."`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-002`, `materialized-input.md:33-38`, `materialized-input.md:40-49`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:33-38,40-49`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-017`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-011`, `rel-011`, `finding-relation-graph.yaml#rel-011`, `finding-011.cause-001`, `finding-011.cause-002`, `finding-011.cause-003`, `finding-011.cause-004`, `finding-017.cause-001`, `finding-017.cause-002`, `finding-017.cause-003`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-012.axiology`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-012`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-012.coverage`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-005`, `Sku `weight` note: warehouse-specific kg or lb convention is used`, `Sku `dims` note: dimensions are a string and units vary by source system`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-012.evolution`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/issue-ledger.yaml#issue-012`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-012.logic`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/finding-relation-graph.yaml#rel-011`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-012.semantics`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-012.structure`, `issue-ledger.yaml#dep-002`, `rel-012`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: semantics, evolution
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - materiality: This weakens the declared purpose because the model is meant to be a common integration basis for WMS/TMS/ERP shipment, inventory, and calculation concepts. If kg/lb or cm/in values can enter the same fields without explicit units and conversion basis, downstream carrier exchange, freight rating, load-limit checks, labels, and validation cannot reliably compare or aggregate those values.
  - root cause: The starting point is that measurement is modeled as a bare number or source-dependent string rather than as a value with unit, source unit authority, and normalization basis. Once the unit is outside the measurement concept, every later aggregate or mapping has to guess the meaning of the value instead of deriving it from the model.
  - causal path: The source model declares Sku.weight as a number using warehouse-specific kg or lb convention, Sku.dims as a string whose unit varies by source system, and Shipment.total_weight as the sum of OrderLine weights without recording unit or conversion criteria. That path allows incompatible source measurements to be combined under one total_weight concept, so existing data and future unit-system additions become hard to compare, migrate, or validate consistently.
  - action: Model weight and dimensions as structured measurements with value and unit, distinguish source units from canonical normalized units, and define conversion rules. Split dimensions into length, width, and height measurements, and make Shipment.total_weight explicit about whether it is a normalized aggregate or source measurement, including calculation unit, calculated_at, source_basis, and preferably the contributing source weights or SKU version basis.

Source finding context: 상태 이름에는 의미 전이가 숨어 있다. 예를 들어 TrackingEvent.delivered가 Shipment.status.delivered와 같은 완료 의미인지, 특정 구간의 도착 이벤트인지, 캐리어별 최종 스캔인지 통합 기준이 판단해 주지 않는다. 이 상태 의미를 연동별로 맡기면 같은 이벤트가 통합마다 다른 Shipment 상태를 만들 수 있다. Source finding context: 캐리어 원천 이벤트와 내부 canonical shipment status를 구분하고, 각 이벤트가 상태 전이를 일으키는 조건과 범위를 기준 문서에 둔다. event_type에는 source_carrier_code/source_event_code를 보존하고, canonical_event_type 또는 status_transition 매핑 테이블을 명시한다. Source finding context: delivered, in_transit, exception 같은 상태의 의미가 연동별로 달라져 배송 완료, 예외 처리, 고객 알림 기준이 불일치할 수 있다. Source finding context: 원천 캐리어 이벤트 의미와 내부 Shipment 상태 의미 사이의 canonical 매핑을 온톨로지 밖 연동 구현으로 위임한다. Source finding context: TrackingEvent.event_type은 캐리어별 상태 코드로 정의된다. Source finding context: Shipment.status와의 매핑은 각 연동에서 알아서 한다고 되어 있다. Source finding context: 주문, 화물, 캐리어 상태는 각 시스템이 독립 관리한다고 되어 있어 공통 상태 의미가 기준 문서 안에서 닫히지 않는다.
  - affected purpose: 배송 상태 모델과 WMS/TMS/ERP/캐리어 통합 상태 의미를 일관되게 제공하는 목적.
  - failure condition: 캐리어 이벤트를 내부 Shipment.status나 Order.fulfillment_status로 반영하는 연동을 둘 이상 구현할 때 각 연동이 자체 매핑을 만든다.
  - impact: delivered, in_transit, exception 같은 상태의 의미가 연동별로 달라져 배송 완료, 예외 처리, 고객 알림 기준이 불일치할 수 있다.
  - root hypothesis: 원천 캐리어 이벤트 의미와 내부 Shipment 상태 의미 사이의 canonical 매핑을 온톨로지 밖 연동 구현으로 위임한다.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-004`, `materialized-input.md TrackingEvent.event_type note`, `materialized-input.md integrity_rules 상태 독립 관리`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md: TrackingEvent.event_type note "캐리어별 상태 코드. 자사 Shipment.status와의 매핑은 각 연동에서 알아서 한다."; integrity_rules "Order.fulfillment_status, Shipment.status, TrackingEvent.event_type는 각 시스템(OMS/TMS/캐리어)이 독립으로 관리한다."`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-018`, `finding-018.cause-001`, `finding-018.cause-002`, `finding-018.cause-003`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-013.axiology`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-013`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-013.coverage`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-004`, `TrackingEvent `event_type` includes exception`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-013.evolution`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/issue-ledger.yaml#issue-013`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-003`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-013.logic`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-013.semantics`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-013.structure`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: semantics
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - materiality: 이 문제는 배송 상태 모델과 WMS/TMS/ERP/캐리어 통합 상태 의미를 일관되게 제공하려는 목적을 직접 약화한다. delivered, in_transit, exception 같은 이름이 캐리어 원천 이벤트인지, 내부 Shipment 완료 상태인지, 고객 알림이나 예외 처리 기준인지 닫힌 기준이 없으면 여러 연동이 각자 매핑을 만들고 동일 이벤트가 서로 다른 완료, 진행, 예외 판단으로 이어진다.
  - root cause: 출발점은 원천 캐리어 이벤트 의미와 내부 Shipment 상태 의미 사이의 canonical 매핑을 온톨로지 밖 연동 구현에 위임한 것이다. TrackingEvent.event_type은 캐리어별 상태 코드로 정의되어 있고 Shipment.status와의 매핑은 각 연동이 처리하도록 되어 있으므로, 공통 상태 의미의 권위가 기준 문서 안에 존재하지 않는다.
  - causal path: 인과 경로는 TrackingEvent.event_type이 캐리어별 상태 코드로 남아 있는 데서 시작한다. 이어서 Shipment.status와의 매핑을 각 연동이 맡게 되고, Order.fulfillment_status, Shipment.status, TrackingEvent.event_type가 각 시스템에서 독립 관리된다는 규칙 때문에 통합 기준 안에서 상태 의미가 닫히지 않는다. 그 결과 TrackingEvent.delivered가 내부 Shipment.status.delivered와 같은 완료인지, 특정 구간 도착 이벤트인지, 캐리어의 최종 스캔인지 연동별로 달라질 수 있다.
  - action: 캐리어 원천 이벤트와 내부 canonical shipment status를 분리해 모델링하고, 각 이벤트가 Shipment.status나 Order.fulfillment_status 전이를 일으키는 조건과 범위를 기준 문서에 명시해야 한다. event_type에는 source_carrier_code와 source_event_code를 보존하고, 별도의 canonical_event_type 또는 status_transition 매핑 테이블을 두어 상태 파생을 연동 구현의 암묵 지식이 아니라 온톨로지의 명시 기준으로 만들어야 한다.

### Conditional Consensus
- issue-005 (narrowed): The ontology has a material fulfillment coverage gap: fulfillment is modeled only from Shipment to Order, so it cannot represent which OrderLine quantity was allocated, picked, shipped, canceled, or backordered in a specific shipment or delivery leg.
  - unresolved disagreement: Deliberation narrowed but did not fully resolve the issue: coverage keeps the issue distinct and high severity because it blocks operational reconstruction, while axiology accepts the issue and action but preserves a medium severity caveat from the value-alignment lens and its relationship to issue-004.
- issue-007 (narrowed): 부분출하, 다중창고 출고, 백오더 같은 분할 충족 패턴을 지원하려면 현재의 Order 단위 fulfills 구조를 유지한 채로는 충분하지 않으며, 충족 권위를 OrderLine 수량 또는 별도 FulfillmentAllocation/ShipmentLine 단위로 내려야 합니다.
  - unresolved disagreement: 진화 관점은 확장 차단 효과 때문에 high severity를 유지하지만, axiology 관점은 동일한 root cause를 수용하면서도 가치 목적만 기준으로 보면 medium severity라는 caveat를 남겼습니다.
- issue-010 (no-deliberation-needed): Inventory quantity and ETA cannot be trusted as integration baseline values until the ontology models which source governs each value, what context it governs, and what evidence explains overrides or supersession.
- issue-014 (narrowed): OrderLine fulfillment is materially incomplete because the ontology describes shipment composition in terms of order lines but provides no structural way to say which OrderLine quantity was fulfilled by which Shipment or DeliveryLeg.
  - unresolved disagreement: Deliberation accepted the root and narrowed the issue to a structural line-level traceability defect, but preserved axiology's caveat that severity is medium under value-purpose criteria while structure keeps high severity for structural completeness.
- issue-002 (no-deliberation-needed): Freight weights, dimensions, and shipment totals are not safe integration values while they remain bare numbers or strings. The ontology must model them as structured measurements with explicit value, unit, source, conversion basis, and relevant package or handling-unit level before it can serve as a unit-consistent WMS/TMS/ERP baseline.
- issue-003 (no-deliberation-needed): The ontology has a material status-model gap: it does not define a versioned canonical mapping from external carrier event codes to internal shipment states, so integrations can derive different meanings for the same shipment lifecycle evidence.
- issue-004 (narrowed): Issue-004 is a material traceability gap: the ontology needs a line-level fulfillment/allocation authority, not only a Shipment-to-Order fulfillment relation, to serve as an order, freight, and delivery integration standard.
  - unresolved disagreement: All participating lenses accepted the missing line-level allocation concept as the actionable root, but severity remains unresolved: axiology, semantics, and logic keep the medium framing, while coverage, evolution, and structure support raising severity because of reconciliation, exception handling, auditability, and future fulfillment patterns.
- issue-006 (no-deliberation-needed): The ontology’s order and shipment lifecycles are materially incomplete because they stop at normal forward progress to delivered while tracking events can already report exceptions. This leaves cancellation, failed delivery, return, reissue, correction, reversal, and archival closure without canonical lifecycle representation.
- issue-012 (no-deliberation-needed): The ontology must treat measurement units as part of the measurement value model. As written, SKU weight, SKU dimensions, and shipment total weight cannot remain stable when warehouses, carriers, regions, or rating engines use different unit conventions.
- issue-013 (no-deliberation-needed): TrackingEvent.event_type과 Shipment.status 사이에 canonical 상태 의미와 전이 기준이 정의되어 있지 않아, 캐리어 이벤트를 내부 배송 상태로 반영하는 통합마다 서로 다른 상태 해석을 만들 수 있다.

### Disagreement
- issue-005: Deliberation narrowed but did not fully resolve the issue: coverage keeps the issue distinct and high severity because it blocks operational reconstruction, while axiology accepts the issue and action but preserves a medium severity caveat from the value-alignment lens and its relationship to issue-004.
- issue-007: 진화 관점은 확장 차단 효과 때문에 high severity를 유지하지만, axiology 관점은 동일한 root cause를 수용하면서도 가치 목적만 기준으로 보면 medium severity라는 caveat를 남겼습니다.
- issue-014: Deliberation accepted the root and narrowed the issue to a structural line-level traceability defect, but preserved axiology's caveat that severity is medium under value-purpose criteria while structure keeps high severity for structural completeness.
- issue-004: All participating lenses accepted the missing line-level allocation concept as the actionable root, but severity remains unresolved: axiology, semantics, and logic keep the medium framing, while coverage, evolution, and structure support raising severity because of reconciliation, exception handling, auditability, and future fulfillment patterns.

### Axiology-Proposed Additional Perspectives
- issue-001 (high): Issue-001 is a high-severity material defect: the ontology cannot safely serve as a WMS/TMS/ERP integration baseline while allocation depends on current or nightly inventory quantity surfaces instead of time-scoped, source-authoritative availability records.
- issue-002 (medium): Freight weights, dimensions, and shipment totals are not safe integration values while they remain bare numbers or strings. The ontology must model them as structured measurements with explicit value, unit, source, conversion basis, and relevant package or handling-unit level before it can serve as a unit-consistent WMS/TMS/ERP baseline.
- issue-003 (medium): The ontology has a material status-model gap: it does not define a versioned canonical mapping from external carrier event codes to internal shipment states, so integrations can derive different meanings for the same shipment lifecycle evidence.
- issue-004 (medium): Issue-004 is a material traceability gap: the ontology needs a line-level fulfillment/allocation authority, not only a Shipment-to-Order fulfillment relation, to serve as an order, freight, and delivery integration standard.

### Purpose Alignment Verification
- issue-001: Use the ontology as the WMS/TMS/ERP integration concept baseline for order, inventory, shipment, and delivery concepts, especially authority and temporality. / Inventory and order fulfillment authority for WMS/TMS/ERP integration.

- issue-010: Use the ontology as a WMS/TMS/ERP integration concept baseline with clear authority for inventory, shipment, ETA, and audit-relevant values. Source finding context: OrderLine / Shipment fulfillment coverage Source finding context: Embedded materialized input: OrderLine attributes comment; relations `fulfills` note Source finding context: For WMS/TMS/ERP integration, fulfillment is not only an order-level state; line quantities may be split, backordered, substituted, or shipped in multiple consignments. Without a line-level fulfillment/allocation concept, the model cannot reconstruct which SKU quantity left in which shipment, weakening reconciliation between order promise, inventory decrement, shipment confirmation, and customer delivery. Source finding context: Add a line-fulfillment concept such as `FulfillmentAllocation` or `ShipmentLine` with refs to `OrderLine`, `Shipment` or `DeliveryLeg`, fulfilled quantity, allocation/pick/ship timestamps, and lifecycle/status for partial, canceled, or backordered quantities. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260718-4065ab62/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-001 Source finding context: WMS/TMS/ERP integration concept baseline for order, inventory, shipment, and delivery concepts. Source finding context: When one order has multiple lines or a line is partially fulfilled across multiple shipments, the ontology has no concept to bind the fulfilled quantity to the actual shipment or leg. Source finding context: Operational reconciliation and exception handling become non-actionable because order status, inventory movement, and shipment evidence cannot be traced at the unit where fulfillment actually occurs. Source finding context: The ontology's fulfillment relation is scoped to `Shipment -> Order` and does not include a line-level fulfillment/allocation entity. Source finding context: `OrderLine` has only `sku_ref` and `qty`, with no fulfilled quantity, shipment reference, or delivery-leg reference. Source finding context: The only fulfillment relation is `Shipment fulfills Order`, and its note explicitly excludes OrderLine-level mapping. Source finding context: This prevents the ontology from covering partial shipment and line-level reconciliation scenarios required by the declared integration purpose.

This is required to make order promise, inventory movement, shipment confirmation, and delivery evidence traceable through the same fulfillment unit.

Source finding context: Extend lifecycle coverage with explicit terminal and post-terminal concepts: canceled/backordered for orders or lines; exception/failed_delivery/returned/reissued for shipments; correction or reversal events; and archival/audit-closed states with rules for how post-delivery events affect status. Source finding context: Operations risk is hidden because exceptions and reversals cannot be represented consistently across OMS, TMS, carrier tracking, and ERP reconciliation. Source finding context: Lifecycle enumerations are limited to forward-progress delivery states and define delivered as final. Source finding context: Order and shipment statuses enumerate only normal forward progress to delivered. Source finding context: Carrier tracking can report `exception`, but shipment status has no exception or recovery state. Source finding context: The ontology therefore does not cover cancellation, failed delivery, returns, reissue, correction, or archival lifecycle phases.
